\displaystyle\|C_{m}^{T}C_{M}\|\leq\sup_{m_{1}}\sum_{m_{2}\leq M}c_{i,m_{1}},c_{i,m_{2}}\leq\tilde{C}^{\prime}(\underline{c})^{-1}\mathcal{S}.

Combining this together we obtain that

P\Big(\big|\sum_{i\leq n}(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{T}\beta)^{2}-\mathbb{E}((\mathbf{X}_{i}^{T}\beta)^{2})\big|\geq t\Big)\leq 2e^{-\frac{Ct\underline{c}}{K\mathcal{S}\|\beta\|^{2}_{2}/p}\min\Big(\frac{t\underline{c}}{\tilde{C}{K}n\|\beta\|^{2}/p},\frac{1}{\tilde{C}^{\prime}}\Big)}.

To prove the second statement, we observe that
(\mathbf{G}_{i}^{T}\beta)
is still a Gaussian process. Hence if we let
\mathbf{N}:=(N_{i})_{i\leq n}
be a vector of standard normal we obtain that
\sum_{i\leq n}(\mathbf{G}_{i}^{T}\beta)^{2}\overset{d}{=}\mathbf{N}^{T}\Sigma_{n}\mathbf{N}
where
\Sigma_{n}:=\rm{Cov}((\mathbf{G}^{T}_{i}\beta)_{i=1}^{n}).
Hence for every
t\geq 0
we have that

P\big(\big|\sum_{i\leq n}(\mathbf{G}_{i}^{T}\beta)^{2}-\mathbb{E}((\mathbf{G}_{i}^{T}\beta)^{2})\big|\geq t\big)\leq P\big(\Big|\mathbf{N}^{T}\Sigma_{n}\mathbf{N}-\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{N}^{T}\Sigma_{n}\mathbf{N})\Big|\geq t\big)

To further bound this the first step is to bound. In this goal we remark that
(\Sigma_{n})_{i,j}=\rm{Cov}(\mathbf{G}_{i}^{T}\beta,\mathbf{G}^{T}_{j}\beta)=\rm{Cov}(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{T}\beta,\mathbf{X}^{T}_{j}\beta)
. Now once again using Lemma 26 of
Austern and Orbanz (2022)
we know that

|\rm{cov}(\beta^{T}\mathbf{X}_{i},\beta^{T}\mathbf{X}_{j})|\leq 4{\beta(i-j)^{\frac{\epsilon}{2+\epsilon}}}\max_{i}\|\beta^{T}\mathbf{X}_{i}\|_{2+\epsilon}^{2}\leq 4\|\beta\|_{2}^{2}/p{\beta(i-j)^{\frac{\epsilon}{2+\epsilon}}}\max_{i,\|\mu\|\leq\sqrt{p}}\|\nu\mathbf{X}_{i}\|_{2+\epsilon}^{2}.

This directly implies that there exists another constant
\tilde{C}_{2}
such that

\|\Sigma_{n}\|^{2}_{F}\leq(\|\beta\|_{2}^{2}/p)^{2}\tilde{C}_{2}\sqrt{n}\mathcal{S}.

We moreover similarly notice that there exists another constant
\tilde{C}_{2}^{\prime}
such that

\|\Sigma_{n}\|\leq\max_{i}\sum_{j\leq n}\rm{cov}(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{T}\beta,\mathbf{X}_{j}^{T}\beta)\leq\|\beta\|_{2}^{2}/p\mathcal{S}\tilde{C}^{\prime}_{2}.

Hence using the Hanson-Wright Inequality (Theorem 1.1 of
Rudelson and Vershynin (2013)
), we obtain that there exists a constant
C_{2}>0
such that

P\big(\Big|\mathbf{N}^{T}\Sigma_{n}\mathbf{N}-\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{N}^{T}\Sigma_{n}\mathbf{N})\Big|\geq t\big)\leq 2e^{-\frac{C_{2}t}{S\|\beta\|_{2}^{2}/p}\min\Big(\frac{t\tilde{C}_{2}}{n\|\beta\|_{2}^{2}/p},\tilde{C}_{2}^{\prime}\Big)}.

\jmlrQED

K.3
Smoothing lemma

Lemma 40
.

For every
\delta>0
, there exists a family of continuous and
\frac{1}{\delta}
-Lipschitz
\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
functions
(h_{\tau;\delta})_{\tau\in\mathbb{R}}
such that, for any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
,

(i)

h_{\tau;\delta}(x)\leq\mathbb{I}\{x<\tau\}\leq h_{\tau+\delta;\delta}(x)
;

(ii)

\big|h_{\tau;\delta}(x)-\mathbb{I}\{x<\tau\}\big|\;\leq\;\mathbb{I}\{x\in[\tau-\delta,\tau)\}
.

Proof of
40
.
We construct
h_{\tau;\delta}
as

h_{\tau;\delta}(x)\;\coloneqq\;\begin{cases}1&\text{ if }x<\tau-\delta\;,\\
\frac{\tau-x}{\delta}&\text{ if }x\in[\tau-\delta,\tau)\;,\\
0&\text{ if }x\geq\tau\;,\end{cases}

which satisfies (i) automatically. To prove (ii), we first note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\{x<\tau-\delta\}\;\leq\;h_{\tau;\delta}(x)\;\leq\;\mathbb{I}\{x<\tau\}\;,

which implies the desired bound that

\displaystyle\big|\,h_{\tau;\delta}(x)-\mathbb{I}\{x<\tau\}\,\big|\leq\,\mathbb{I}\{x\in[\tau-\delta,\tau)\}\;.

\jmlrQED

K.4
Additional Lindeberg Lemma

Lemma 41
.

Suppose that
(X_{i})
are independent, centered random variables. We obtain that there is an absolute constant
\kappa>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\absolutevalue{\E f\l(\sum_{j\le n}X_j\r) - \E f\l(\sum_{j\le n}Z_j\r) }

(93)

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{\kappa n}{\epsilon^{2}}\Big)^{1/3}\max_{j\leq n}\|X_{j}\|_{3},

(94)

where
(Z_{i})
is an independent sequence of independent and centered Gaussian random variables.

Proof
Let
f\in\mathcal{F}
, and let
\epsilon>0
. Define
f_{\epsilon}(u)=\frac{1}{4\epsilon^{2}}\int_{u-\epsilon}^{u+\epsilon}\int_{t-\epsilon}^{t+\epsilon}f(y)dydt
. We remark that
f_{\epsilon}
is three times differentiable and as
f
is Lipschitz we have
\sup_{x}|f_{\epsilon}(x)-f(x)|\leq 2\epsilon.

Write

X_{j}(t):=\sqrt{t}X_{j}+\sqrt{1-t}G_{j}

and

X^{j,0}_{m}(t):=\begin{cases}0\text{~if~}m=j\\
X_{m}(t)~~\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Using the fundamental theorem of calculus we obtain that

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{E}(f_{\epsilon}(\sum_{j}X_{j})-\mathbb{E}(f_{\epsilon}(\sum_{j}Z_{j}))\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1}\Big|\partial_{t}\mathbb{E}(f_{\epsilon}(\sum_{j}X_{j}(t)))\Big|dt

\displaystyle\overset{(d_{1})}{\leq}\int_{0}^{1}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big(f^{\prime}_{\epsilon}(\sum_{j}X_{j}(t))\sum_{j}\frac{X_{j}}{2\sqrt{t}}-\frac{Z_{j}}{2\sqrt{1-t}}\Big)\Big|dt

\displaystyle\overset{(d_{2})}{\leq}\int_{0}^{1}\sum_{j\leq n}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big(f^{\prime}_{\epsilon}(\sum X^{j,0}(t))\big[\frac{X_{j}}{2\sqrt{t}}-\frac{Z_{j}}{2\sqrt{1-t}}\big]\Big)\Big|dt

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{1}\sum_{j\leq n}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big(f^{\prime\prime}_{\epsilon}(\sum X^{j,0}(t))\big[\frac{X_{j}}{2\sqrt{t}}-\frac{Z_{j}}{2\sqrt{1-t}}\big]\big[X_{j}\sqrt{t}+Z_{j}\sqrt{1-t}\big]\Big)\Big|dt

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{1}\sum_{j\leq n}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big(f^{(3)}_{\epsilon}(\sum_{j}\tilde{X}_{j}(t))\big[\frac{X_{j}}{2\sqrt{t}}-\frac{Z_{j}}{2\sqrt{1-t}}\big]\big[X_{j}\sqrt{t}+Z_{j}\sqrt{1-t}\big]^{2}\Big)\Big|dt

\displaystyle\leq(a)+(b)+(c)

where
\sum\tilde{X}_{j}(t)\in[\sum X_{j}(t),\sum X^{j,0}(t)]
and where
d_{1}
is a result of the product law and
d_{2}
of Taylor’s expansion.
Using the independence between
X^{j,0}(t)
and
X_{j}
and
Z_{j}
and the fact that those latter are centered, we obtain that
(a)=0
. Similarly, we notice for all
j\leq n
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big(f^{\prime\prime}_{\epsilon}(\sum X^{j,0}(t))\big[\frac{X_{j}}{2\sqrt{t}}-\frac{Z_{j}}{2\sqrt{1-t}}\big]\big[X_{j}\sqrt{t}+Z_{j}\sqrt{1-t}\big]\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big(f^{\prime\prime}_{\epsilon}(\sum X^{j,0}(t))\Big)\mathbb{E}\Big(\big[\frac{X_{j}}{2\sqrt{t}}-\frac{Z_{j}}{2\sqrt{1-t}}\big]\big[X_{j}\sqrt{t}+Z_{j}\sqrt{1-t}\big]\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big(f^{\prime\prime}_{\epsilon}(\sum X^{j,0}(t))\Big)\mathbb{E}\Big(\big[\frac{X_{j}^{2}}{2}-\frac{Z_{j}^{2}}{2}\big]\Big)=0.

Hence
(b)=0.

Finally we can note that
\|f_{\epsilon}^{(3)}\|\leq\frac{4}{\epsilon^{2}}
. Hence, thanks to Jensen inequality we know that there exists absolute constants
C,C_{2}>0
such that

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big(f^{(3)}_{\epsilon}(\sum_{j}\tilde{X}_{j}(t))\big[\frac{X_{j}}{2\sqrt{t}}-\frac{Z_{j}}{2\sqrt{1-t}}\big]\big[X_{j}\sqrt{t}+Z_{j}\sqrt{1-t}\big]^{2}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{\epsilon^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big(\Big|\frac{X_{j}}{2\sqrt{t}}-\frac{Z_{j}}{2\sqrt{1-t}}\Big|\big[X_{j}\sqrt{t}+Z_{j}\sqrt{1-t}\big]^{2}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\epsilon^{2}}\max(\|X_{j}\|_{3}^{3},\|Z_{j}\|_{3}^{3})\Big[\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-t}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\epsilon^{2}}\max(\|X_{j}\|_{3}^{3},\sqrt{3}^{3}\|X_{j}\|_{2}^{3})\Big[\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-t}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{2}}{\epsilon^{2}}\|X_{j}\|_{3}^{3}\Big[\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-t}}\Big]

where we used the fact that if
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
then
\|Z\|_{3}\leq\sqrt{3}
coupled with the fact that
\|Z_{j}\|_{2}=\|X_{j}\|_{2}.
Hence we obtain that there is an absolute constant
\kappa>0
such that

\displaystyle(c)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\kappa n}{\epsilon^{2}}\max_{j\leq n}\|X_{j}\|_{3}^{3}

(95)

This gives us the desired result by choosing
\epsilon:=\Big(\kappa n\Big)^{1/3}\max_{j\leq n}\|X_{j}\|_{3}.

\jmlrQED

K.5
Asymptotic normality under local dependency assumption

Lemma 42
.

Suppose that
(X_{i})
are centered random vectors such that the array
(X_{i,\ell})_{i,\ell}
is locally dependent. Write
B_{i,\ell}
the dependency neighborhood of the entry
X_{i,\ell}
. For a fixed
q\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\mathcal{F}_{q}
be the class of
\mathbb{R}^{q}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
continuously differentiable functions with
\|f\|_{\infty}\leq 1
and
\|\,\|\partial f\|\,\|_{\infty}\leq 1
. Then there is a constant
C_{q}>0
that depends only on
q
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in\mathcal{F}_{q}\\
\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{q}\in\mathcal{S}^{k-1}\end{subarray}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\beta_{11},\ldots,\beta_{kq})\\
\in\mathcal{S}_{p}^{kq}\end{subarray}}\absolutevalue{
\E\textsf{f}
\begin{pmatrix}
\sum_{i=1}^k \theta_{1i} X_i^\intercal\beta_{1i}
\\ \vdots\\
\sum_{i=1}^k \theta_{qi} X_i^\intercal\beta_{qi}
\end{pmatrix}
-
\E\textsf{f}
\begin{pmatrix}
\sum_{i=1}^k \theta_{1i} G_i^\intercal\beta_{1i}
\\ \vdots\\
\sum_{i=1}^k \theta_{qi} G_i^\intercal\beta_{qi}
\end{pmatrix}
}

\displaystyle\hskip 150.00023pt\leq C_{q}\,\Big(k^{2}\,p^{3/2-r}\,\max_{i,\ell}|B_{i,\ell}|^{3/2}\,\max_{i,\ell}\|X_{i,\ell}\|^{3}_{L_{3}}\Big)^{1/(2q+1)}\;,

(96)

where
\mathcal{S}^{k-1}
denotes the unit sphere in
\mathbb{R}^{k}
and
(G_{i})
is an independent sequence of mean-zero Gaussian vectors chosen such that
\text{cov}(G_{i,l},G_{j,m})=\text{cov}(X_{i,l},X_{j,m})
.

Proof
Fix
f\in\mathcal{F}_{q}
. Let
\epsilon>0
and define a smoothed version of
f
,

\displaystyle f_{\epsilon}(u)\;\coloneqq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{(2\epsilon)^{2q}}$}}\mint_{u_{1}-\epsilon}^{u_{1}+\epsilon}\cdots\mint_{u_{q}-\epsilon}^{u_{q}+\epsilon}\;\mint_{t_{1}-\epsilon}^{t_{1}+\epsilon}\cdots\mint_{t_{q}-\epsilon}^{t_{q}+\epsilon}f(y)\;dy_{1}\ldots dy_{q}\;dt_{1}\ldots dt_{q}\;\;.

Note that
f_{\epsilon}
is thrice differentiable and, as
f
is Lipschitz, we have
\sup_{x}|f_{\epsilon}(x)-f(x)|\leq 6\epsilon\sqrt{q}
. Write

\displaystyle X_{j}(t)\coloneqq\sqrt{t}X_{j}+\sqrt{1-t}G_{j}\;,\;\;a_{jl}\coloneqq\big(\theta_{1j}\beta_{1jl}\,,\,\ldots\,,\,\theta_{qj}\beta_{qjl}\big)\in\mathbb{R}^{q}\;,\;\;A_{j}\coloneqq\begin{pmatrix}\leftarrow&a_{j1}^{\intercal}&\rightarrow\\
&\vdots&\\
\leftarrow&a_{jp}^{\intercal}&\rightarrow\\
\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times q}\;,

and

\big(X_{j,i,\ell,0}(t)\big)_{m}\coloneqq\begin{cases}0\text{~if~}(j,m)\in B_{i,\ell}\\
X_{j,m}(t)~~\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}.

Using the fundamental theorem of calculus we obtain that

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{
\E f_\epsilon\begin{pmatrix}
\sum_{i=1}^k \theta_{1i} X_i^\intercal\beta_{1i}
\\ \vdots\\
\sum_{i=1}^k \theta_{qi} X_i^\intercal\beta_{qi}
\end{pmatrix}
-
\E f_\epsilon\begin{pmatrix}
\sum_{i=1}^k \theta_{1i} G_i^\intercal\beta_{1i}
\\ \vdots\\
\sum_{i=1}^k \theta_{qi} G_i^\intercal\beta_{qi}
\end{pmatrix}
}\;=\;\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[f_{\epsilon}\Big(\msum_{j\leq n}X_{j}^{\intercal}A_{j}\Big)\Big]-\mathbb{E}\Big[f_{\epsilon}\Big(\msum_{j\leq n}G_{j}^{\intercal}A_{j}\Big)\Big]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1}\Big|\partial_{t}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[f_{\epsilon}\Big(\msum_{j}\ X_{j}^{\intercal}(t)\,A_{j}\Big)\Big]\Big|\,dt

\displaystyle\overset{(d_{1})}{\leq}\int_{0}^{1}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\partial f_{\epsilon}(\msum_{j}X_{j}^{\intercal}(t)A_{j})\msum_{i\leq k}A_{i}^{\intercal}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ X_i}{2\sqrt{t}}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{G_i}{2\sqrt{1-t}}$}}\Big)\Big]\Big|dt

\displaystyle\overset{(d_{2})}{\leq}\int_{0}^{1}\,\Big|\msum_{i\leq k}\msum_{\ell\leq p}\mathbb{E}\Big[\partial f_{\epsilon}\Big(\msum_{j}X_{j,i,\ell,0}^{\intercal}(t)A_{j}\Big)^{\intercal}a_{il}\,\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{X_{i,\ell}}{2\sqrt{t}}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{G_{i,\ell}}{2\sqrt{1-t}}$}}\Big)\Big]\Big|dt

\displaystyle+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\int_{0}^{1}\Big|\msum_{i\leq k}\msum_{\ell\leq p}\msum_{(\tilde{i},\tilde{\ell})\in B_{i,\ell}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(X_{\tilde{i},\tilde{\ell}}\sqrt{t}+G_{\tilde{i},\tilde{\ell}}\sqrt{1-t}\big)a_{\tilde{i},\tilde{l}}^{\intercal}\,\partial^{2}f_{\epsilon}\Big(\msum_{j}X_{j,i,\ell,0}^{T}(t)A_{j}\Big)\,a_{il}

\displaystyle\hskip 200.0003pt\,\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{X_{i,l}}{2\sqrt{t}}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{G_{i,l}}{2\sqrt{1-t}}$}}\Big)\Big]\Big|dt

\displaystyle+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{6 \epsilon^q}$}}\int_{0}^{1}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\,\Big\|\sum_{i\leq k}\sum_{\ell\leq p}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\tilde{i},\tilde{\ell}),(\tilde{i}_{2},\tilde{\ell}_{2})\\
\in B_{i,\ell}\end{subarray}}\hskip-6.0pt\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{X_{i,l}}{2\sqrt{t}}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{G_{i,l}}{2\sqrt{1-t}}$}}\Big)\,\big(X_{\tilde{i},\tilde{\ell}}\sqrt{t}+G_{\tilde{i},\tilde{\ell}}\sqrt{1-t}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 180.00027pt\,\big(X_{\tilde{i}_{2},\tilde{\ell}_{2}}\sqrt{t}+G_{\tilde{i}_{2},\tilde{\ell}_{2}}\hskip-4.0pt\sqrt{1-t}\big)\,(a_{il}\otimes a_{\tilde{i}\tilde{\ell}}\otimes a_{\tilde{i}_{2},\tilde{\ell}_{2}})\Big\|\,\Big]\,dt

\displaystyle\leq(a)+(b)+(c)\;,

where
\tilde{X}_{j,i,l}(t)\in[X_{j}(t),X_{j,i,l,0}(t)]
. In
(d_{1})
, we have used the product rule; in
(d_{2})
, we have used a third-order Taylor expansion together with the bound that
\|\partial^{3}f_{\epsilon}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon^{q}}
. Using the independence between
X_{j,i,\ell,0}(t)
and
X_{i,\ell}
and
G_{i,\ell}
and the fact that these variables are centered, we obtain that for all
i\leq k
and
\ell\leq p
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\partial f_{\epsilon}\Big(\msum_{j}X_{j,i,\ell,0}^{\intercal}(t)\Theta^{\beta}_{j}\Big)(\Theta^{\beta}_{i})^{\intercal}\,\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{X_{i,\ell}}{2\sqrt{t}}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{G_{i,\ell}}{2\sqrt{1-t}}$}}\Big)\Big]\;=\;0\;.

Hence we know that
(a)=0
. Similarly we notice that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(X_{\tilde{i},\tilde{\ell}}\sqrt{t}+G_{\tilde{i},\tilde{\ell}}\sqrt{1-t}\big)\big(\Theta^{\beta,(\tilde{l})}_{\tilde{i}}\big)^{\intercal}\,\partial^{2}f_{\epsilon}\Big(\msum_{j}X_{j,i,\ell,0}^{T}(t)\Theta^{\beta}_{j}\Big)\,(\Theta^{\beta}_{i})^{\intercal}\,\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{X_i}{2\sqrt{t}}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{G_i}{2\sqrt{1-t}}$}}\Big)\Big]\;=\;0\;,

where we use the independence between
(X_{i,j})
and
(G_{i,j})
to ignore cross terms and the fact that
\mathbb{E}(X_{i,\ell}X_{\tilde{i},\tilde{\ell}})=\mathbb{E}(G_{i,\ell}G_{\tilde{i},\tilde{\ell}})
. Hence
(b)=0.
Finally to handle
(c)
, we see that

\displaystyle\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\,\Big\|\sum_{i\leq k}\sum_{\ell\leq p}\sum_{(\tilde{i},\tilde{\ell}),(\tilde{i}_{2},\tilde{\ell}_{2})\in B_{i,\ell}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{X_{i,l}}{2\sqrt{t}}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{G_{i,l}}{2\sqrt{1-t}}$}}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 100.00015pt\,\times\big(X_{\tilde{i},\tilde{\ell}}\sqrt{t}+G_{\tilde{i},\tilde{\ell}}\sqrt{1-t}\big)\,\big(X_{\tilde{i}_{2},\tilde{\ell}_{2}}\sqrt{t}+G_{\tilde{i}_{2},\tilde{\ell}_{2}}\sqrt{1-t}\big)\,(a_{il}\otimes a_{\tilde{i}\tilde{\ell}}\otimes a_{\tilde{i}_{2},\tilde{\ell}_{2}})\Big\|\,\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\;\msum_{s,\tilde{s},\tilde{s}_{2}\leq q}\Big(\sum_{i\leq k}\sum_{\ell\leq p}\sum_{(\tilde{i},\tilde{\ell}),(\tilde{i}_{2},\tilde{\ell}_{2})\in B_{i,\ell}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \theta_{si} \beta_{sil} X_{i,l}}{2\sqrt{t}}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \theta_{si} \beta_{sil} G_{i,l}}{2\sqrt{1-t}}$}}\Big)\,\theta_{\tilde{s}\tilde{i}}\beta_{\tilde{s}\tilde{i}\tilde{l}}\big(X_{\tilde{i},\tilde{\ell}}\sqrt{t}+G_{\tilde{i},\tilde{\ell}}\sqrt{1-t}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 150.00023pt\,\theta_{\tilde{s}_{2}\tilde{i}_{2}}\beta_{\tilde{s}_{2}\tilde{i}_{2}\tilde{l}_{2}}\big(X_{\tilde{i}_{2},\tilde{\ell}_{2}}\sqrt{t}+G_{\tilde{i}_{2},\tilde{\ell}_{2}}\sqrt{1-t}\big)\Big)^{2}\;\Big)^{1/2}\Big]

\displaystyle\overset{(d_{3})}{\leq}\msum_{s,\tilde{s},\tilde{s}_{2}\leq q}\msum_{i\leq k}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{\ell\leq p}\sum_{(\tilde{i},\tilde{\ell}),(\tilde{i}_{2},\tilde{\ell}_{2})\in B_{i,\ell}}\,|\theta_{si}\beta_{sil}|\,\Big|\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ X_{i,l}}{2\sqrt{t}}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ G_{i,l}}{2\sqrt{1-t}}$}}\Big|\,|\theta_{\tilde{s}\tilde{i}}\beta_{\tilde{s}\tilde{i}\tilde{l}}|\,\big|X_{\tilde{i},\tilde{\ell}}\sqrt{t}+G_{\tilde{i},\tilde{\ell}}\sqrt{1-t}\big|

\displaystyle\hskip 230.00035pt\,|\theta_{\tilde{s}_{2}\tilde{i}_{2}}\beta_{\tilde{s}_{2}\tilde{i}_{2}\tilde{l}_{2}}|\,\big|X_{\tilde{i}_{2},\tilde{\ell}_{2}}\sqrt{t}+G_{\tilde{i}_{2},\tilde{\ell}_{2}}\sqrt{1-t}\big|\Big]

\displaystyle\overset{(d_{4})}{\leq}\msum_{s,\tilde{s},\tilde{s}_{2}\leq q}\msum_{i\leq k}\msum_{\ell\leq p}\msum_{(\tilde{i},\tilde{\ell}),(\tilde{i}_{2},\tilde{\ell}_{2})\in B_{i,\ell}}\,|\beta_{sil}|\,|\beta_{\tilde{s}\tilde{i}\tilde{l}}|\,|\beta_{\tilde{s}_{2}\tilde{i}_{2}\tilde{l}_{2}}|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ X_{i,l}}{2\sqrt{t}}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ G_{i,l}}{2\sqrt{1-t}}$}}\Big|\,\big|X_{\tilde{i},\tilde{\ell}}\sqrt{t}+G_{\tilde{i},\tilde{\ell}}\sqrt{1-t}\big|\big|X_{\tilde{i}_{2},\tilde{\ell}_{2}}\sqrt{t}+G_{\tilde{i}_{2},\tilde{\ell}_{2}}\sqrt{1-t}\big|\Big]

\displaystyle\overset{(d_{5})}{\leq}\msum_{s,\tilde{s},\tilde{s}_{2}\leq q}\msum_{i\leq k}\msum_{\ell\leq p}\msum_{(\tilde{i},\tilde{\ell}),(\tilde{i}_{2},\tilde{\ell}_{2})\in B_{i,\ell}}\,|\beta_{sil}|\,|\beta_{\tilde{s}\tilde{i}\tilde{l}}|\,|\beta_{\tilde{s}_{2}\tilde{i}_{2}\tilde{l}_{2}}|\,\times\,\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2\sqrt{t}}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2\sqrt{1-t}}$}}\Big)(1+\sqrt{3})^{2}\max_{i,\ell}\|X_{i,\ell}\|^{3}_{L_{3}}\;.

In
(d_{3})
, we have moved the summations outside a squareroot and an absolute value; in
(d_{4})
, we have noted that
\theta\in\mathcal{S}^{k-1}
; in
(d_{5})
, we have used that for
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
,
\|Z\|_{L_{3}}\leq\sqrt{3}
. Now let
(\beta_{\rm{mix}})_{s}\coloneqq(|(\beta_{\rm{mix}})_{s11}|,\ldots,|(\beta_{\rm{mix}})_{skp}|)\in\mathbb{R}^{kp}
and

M^{(i,\ell)}\in\mathbb{R}^{kp\times kp}
be a matrix with entries
M^{(i,\ell)}_{(i^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}),(i^{\prime\prime},\ell^{\prime\prime})}=\mathbb{I}\{(i^{\prime},\ell^{\prime})\in B_{i,\ell}\}\,\mathbb{I}\{(i^{\prime\prime},\ell^{\prime\prime})\in B_{i,\ell}\}
. Also recall that by the definition of
\mathcal{S}_{p}
in (
4
), there are fixed constants
L,r>0
such that
\|\beta_{si}\|_{\infty}\leq Lp^{1/2-r}
and
\|\beta_{si}\|_{2}\leq Lp^{1/2}
for all
s\in q,i\leq k
. Then

\displaystyle\msum_{\ell\leq p}\msum_{(\tilde{i},\tilde{\ell}),(\tilde{i}_{2},\tilde{\ell}_{2})\in B_{i,\ell}}|\beta_{sil}|\,|\beta_{\tilde{s}\tilde{i}\tilde{l}}|\,|\beta_{\tilde{s}_{2}\tilde{i}_{2}\tilde{l}_{2}}|\;\leq

\displaystyle\;Lp^{1/2-r}\,\msum_{\ell\leq p}\;\msum_{(\tilde{i},\tilde{\ell}),(\tilde{i}_{2},\tilde{\ell}_{2})\in B_{i,\ell}}\,|\beta_{\tilde{s}\tilde{i}\tilde{l}}|\,|\beta_{\tilde{s}_{2}\tilde{i}_{2}\tilde{l}_{2}}|

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;Lp^{1/2-r}\,\msum_{\ell\leq p}(\beta_{\rm{mix}})_{\tilde{s}}^{\intercal}M^{(i,\ell)}(\beta_{\rm{mix}})_{\tilde{s}_{2}}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;Lp^{1/2-r}\,\|(\beta_{\rm{mix}})_{\tilde{s}}\|\,\|(\beta_{\rm{mix}})_{\tilde{s}_{2}}\|\,\big\|\msum_{\ell\leq p}M^{(i,\ell)}\big\|_{op}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;L^{3}kp^{3/2-r}\,\big\|\msum_{\ell\leq p}M^{(i,\ell)}\big\|_{op}

In the last line, we have noted that
\|(\beta_{\rm{mix}})_{\tilde{s}}\|^{2}=\sum_{\tilde{i}\leq k}\|(\beta_{\rm{mix}})_{\tilde{s}\tilde{i}}\|^{2}\leq L^{2}kp
. Now observe that the
(i^{\prime},\ell^{\prime})
-th column of the matrix
\sum_{\ell\leq p}M^{(i,\ell)}
is given by

\displaystyle\Big(\msum_{\ell\leq p}\mathbb{I}\{(i^{\prime},\ell^{\prime})\in B_{i,\ell}\}\,\mathbb{I}\{(i^{\prime\prime},\ell^{\prime\prime})\in B_{i,\ell}\}\Big)_{i^{\prime\prime}\leq k,\ell^{\prime\prime}\leq p}\;.

Since
|B_{i,\ell}|\leq\max_{i,\ell}|B_{i,\ell}|
, the column has at most
\max_{i,\ell}|B_{i,\ell}|
non-zero entries. For each
(i^{\prime\prime},\ell^{\prime\prime})
, since the dependency neighborhood induces an equivalence relation and
|B_{i^{\prime\prime},\ell^{\prime\prime}}|\leq\max_{i,\ell}|B_{i,\ell}|
, the
(i^{\prime\prime},\ell^{\prime\prime})
-th entry cannot exceed
\max_{i,\ell}|B_{i,\ell}|
. In other words, the
l_{2}
-norm of each column vector of
\sum_{\ell\leq p}M^{(i,\ell)}
cannot exceed
\sqrt{\max_{i,\ell}|B_{i,\ell}|\times\max_{i,\ell}|B_{i,\ell}|^{2}}=\max_{i,\ell}|B_{i,\ell}|^{3/2}
, which implies

\displaystyle\msum_{\ell\leq p}\msum_{(\tilde{i},\tilde{\ell}),(\tilde{i}_{2},\tilde{\ell}_{2})\in B_{i,\ell}}|\beta_{sil}|\,|\beta_{\tilde{s}\tilde{i}\tilde{l}}|\,|\beta_{\tilde{s}_{2}\tilde{i}_{2}\tilde{l}_{2}}|\;\leq

\displaystyle\;L^{3}kp^{3/2-r}\,\max_{i,\ell}|B_{i,\ell}|^{3/2}\ ;.

Combining the bounds, we obtain that for some constant
C_{q}>0
depending only on
q
,

\displaystyle(c)

\displaystyle\leq\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{C_q}{\epsilon^{2q}}$}}\,k^{2}\,p^{3/2-r}\,\max_{i,\ell}|B_{i,\ell}|^{3/2}\,\max_{i,\ell}\|X_{i,\ell}\|^{3}_{L_{3}}\;.

This gives us the desired result by choosing
\epsilon:=(C_{q}\,k^{2}\,p^{3/2-r}\,\max_{i,\ell}|B_{i,\ell}|^{3/2}\,\max_{i,\ell}\|X_{i,\ell}\|^{3}_{L_{3}})^{1/(2q+1)}.

\jmlrQED

K.6
Polynomial Approximation Properties

In this section we discuss some of the properties of our polynomial approximation that are used in the proof of our main theorem and also
Lemma

27
.

Lemma 43
.

Let
\alpha,\delta,\gamma,\tau>0
. Then there exists finite
\textnormal{{D}}=\textnormal{{D}}(k,\alpha,\tau)
such that, if we define

\displaystyle Q_{\textnormal{{D}}}(x):=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\textnormal{{D}}}(1-x)^{\ell},\quad\quad R_{\textnormal{{D}}}(x):=\frac{1}{x}-Q_{\textnormal{{D}}}(x),

then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{(i,k)}\left[R_{\textnormal{{D}}}\left(\langle e^{-\alpha\sum_{j\in\mathcal{B}_{i}}\omega_{j}\ell(\eta_{j},U_{j}^{\intercal}\beta)}\rangle_{i,k}\right)^{2}\right]<\tau.

Proof
For
t>0
, define the event

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{t}:=\left\{\max_{j\in\mathcal{B}_{i}}\absolutevalue{U_j^\intercal\beta}\leq t\right\}=\bigcap_{j\in\mathcal{B}_{i}}\left\{\absolutevalue{U_j^\intercal\beta}\leq t\right\}.

Then we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{A}_{t}^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{=}}\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{j\in\mathcal{B}_{i}}\left\{\absolutevalue{U_j^\intercal\beta}>t\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\leq}}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{B}_{i}}\mathbb{P}\left(\absolutevalue{U_j^\intercal\beta}>t\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iii)}}{{\leq}}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{B}_{i}}2e^{-ct^{2}/\textsf{C}_{1}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\textsf{C}_{2}ke^{-ct^{2}}.

(97)

where
(i)
is via De Morgan’s Law,
(ii)
is via a union bound, and
(iii)
is from (
60
) which bounds the sub-Gaussian norm of each
\absolutevalue{U_j^\intercal\beta}
along with Proposition 2.5.2 of
Vershynin (2018)
. We can then say that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{(i,k)}\left[R_{\textnormal{{D}}}\left(\langle e^{-\alpha\sum_{j\in\mathcal{B}_{i}}\omega_{j}\ell(\eta_{j},U_{j}^{\intercal}\beta)}\rangle_{i,k}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\mathbb{E}_{(i,k)}\left\langle R_{\textnormal{{D}}}\left(e^{-\alpha\sum_{j\in\mathcal{B}_{i}}\omega_{j}\ell(\eta_{j},U_{j}^{\intercal}\beta)}\right)^{2}\right\rangle_{i,k}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{=}}\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{(i,k)}\left[R_{\textnormal{{D}}}\left(e^{-\alpha\sum_{j\in\mathcal{B}_{i}}\omega_{j}\ell(\eta_{j},U_{j}^{\intercal}\beta)}\right)^{2}\right]\right\rangle_{i,k}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iii)}}{{=}}\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{(i,k)}\left[R_{\textnormal{{D}}}\left(e^{-\alpha\sum_{j\in\mathcal{B}_{i}}\omega_{j}\ell(\eta_{j},U_{j}^{\intercal}\beta)}\right)^{2}\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}\right]\right\rangle_{i,k}

(98)

\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt+\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{(i,k)}\left[R_{\textnormal{{D}}}\left(e^{-\alpha\sum_{j\in\mathcal{B}_{i}}\omega_{j}\ell(\eta_{j},U_{j}^{\intercal}\beta)}\right)^{2}\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{c}}\right]\right\rangle_{i,k}

(99)

where
(i)
is because
R_{\textsf{D}}^{2}
is convex, as it is the square of a positive, convex function,
(ii)
is because
\mathbb{E}_{(i,k)}[\ ]
and
\langle\ \rangle_{i,k}
commute, and
(iii)
is the Law of Total Probability. We first bound the term inside the expectation of (
99
) as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{(i,k)}\left[R_{\textnormal{{D}}}\left(e^{-\alpha\sum_{j\in\mathcal{B}_{i}}\omega_{j}\ell(\eta_{j},U_{j}^{\intercal}\beta)}\right)^{2}\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{c}}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\mathbb{E}\left[R_{\textnormal{{D}}}\left(e^{-\alpha\sum_{j\in\mathcal{B}_{i}}\omega_{j}\ell(\eta_{j},U_{j}^{\intercal}\beta)}\right)^{4}\right]^{1/2}\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{A}_{t}^{c}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\leq}}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{4\alpha\sum_{j\in\mathcal{B}_{i}}\omega_{j}\ell(\eta_{j},U_{j}^{\intercal}\beta)}\right]^{1/2}\textsf{C}_{3}\sqrt{k}e^{-ct^{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iii)}}{{\leq}}e^{\textsf{C}_{4}k^{2}\alpha^{2}}\textsf{C}_{3}\sqrt{k}e^{-ct^{2}}

\displaystyle=\textsf{C}(k,\alpha)e^{-ct^{2}},

(100)

where
(i)
is via Cauchy-Schwarz and block dependence,
(ii)
is via (
97
) and the fact that

\displaystyle Q_{\textsf{D}}(x)>0\implies R_{\textsf{D}}(x)=\frac{1}{x}-Q_{\textsf{D}}(x)<\frac{1}{x}

for
x\in(0,1)
, and
(iii)
is via
(
62
)
. Thus, if we choose
t
sufficiently large, namely

\displaystyle t>\sqrt{\frac{1}{c}\log\left(\frac{2\textsf{C}(k,\alpha)}{\tau}\right)},

then (
100
) yields that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{(i,k)}\left[R_{\textnormal{{D}}}\left(e^{-\alpha\sum_{j\in\mathcal{B}_{i}}\omega_{j}\ell(\eta_{j},U_{j}^{\intercal}\beta)}\right)^{2}\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{c}}\right]\leq\frac{\tau}{2}.

(101)

For (
98
), we know that since the event
\mathcal{A}_{t}
occurs in this case, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{t}\implies\sum_{j\in\mathcal{B}_{i}}\omega_{j}\ell_{j}(\beta)\leq\sum_{j\in\mathcal{B}_{i}}\absolutevalue{U_j^\intercal\beta}+1\leq k(t+1),

and so this forces that the argument of
R_{\textsf{D}}
satisfies

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\alpha\sum_{j\in\mathcal{B}_{i}}\omega_{j}\ell_{j}(\beta)\right)\in[e^{-\alpha k(t+1)},1].

By definition of
Q_{\textsf{D}}(x)
being the power series of
\frac{1}{x}
with radius of convergence equal to 1, there must exist
\textsf{D}(k,\alpha,\tau)
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[e^{-\alpha k(t+1)},1]}\absolutevalue{R_{\textsf D}(x)}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\tau}{2}}.

This means that the term inside the expectation of (
98
) may be bounded as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{(i,k)}\left[R_{\textnormal{{D}}}\left(e^{-\alpha\sum_{j\in\mathcal{B}_{i}}\omega_{j}\ell(\eta_{j},U_{j}^{\intercal}\beta)}\right)^{2}\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}\right]\leq\frac{\tau}{2},

(102)

and so the result follows from this choice of
D
by combining (
101
) and (
102
).

\jmlrQED

K.7
Properties of sub-Gaussian Vectors

Lemma 44
.

Let
Y
be a sub-Gaussian vector in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with sub-Gaussian constant
K
. Write
\Sigma_{Y}:=\text{\rm Var}(Y)
. Then there exists
\textnormal{{C}}>0
such that

\|\Sigma_{Y}\|_{\textnormal{op}}\leq\textnormal{{C}}\textnormal{{K}}^{2}.

Proof
Define
Z:=Y-\mathbb{E}[Y]
, which by Lemma 2.6.8. of
Vershynin (2018)
is still sub-Gaussian with

\displaystyle\|Z\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq\textsf{C}_{1}\textsf{K}

for some fixed
\textsf{C}_{1}>0
. Now, let
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. We first know by
9
that since
Z
is sub-Gaussian with constant
\textsf{C}_{1}\textsf{K}
,
Z^{\intercal}v
must also be sub-Gaussian with constant at most
\textsf{C}_{1}\textsf{K}\|v\|
. We observe that

\displaystyle v^{\intercal}\Sigma_{Y}v\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{=}}\text{\rm Var}(Z^{\intercal}v)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\leq}}\textsf{C}_{2}\textsf{K}^{2}\|v\|^{2},

where
(i)
is because
Y
and
Z
share the same covariance matrix, and the inequality in
(ii)
is via Proposition 2.5.2 of
Vershynin (2018)
. This lets us conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{v^{\intercal}\Sigma_{Y}v}{\|v\|^{2}}\leq\textsf{C}_{2}\textsf{K}^{2},

and since this holds for all
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, it holds for the supremum, which exactly defines the operator norm as
\Sigma_{Y}
is necessarily positive semi-definite.

\jmlrQED

Appendix L
Proofs for the dependent CGMT

In this section, we prove
13
, which recovers
5
directly, and
7
. The proof recipe is similar to that of a standard CGMT: We start by proving a Gaussian min-max theorem (GMT) on discrete sets in
45
, proceed to extend it to compact sets in
46
, and then prove the results in
13
.
7
then follows directly from
13
(ii).

As with the standard CGMT, the Gaussian min-max theorem (GMT) on discrete sets is proved for a surrogate optimization problem. Let
(\xi_{l})_{l\leq M}
be a collection of univariate standard Gaussians independent of
\mathbf{H}
, and define

\displaystyle\Psi^{\xi}_{\mathcal{S}_{w},\mathcal{S}_{u}}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\min_{w\in\mathcal{S}_{w}}\,\max_{u\in\mathcal{S}_{u}}\,L^{\xi}_{\Psi}(w,u)\;,\qquad\text{ where }\quad L^{\xi}_{\Psi}(w,u)\;\coloneqq\;w^{\intercal}\mathbf{H}u+\msum_{l=1}^{M}\xi_{l}\,\|w\|_{\Sigma^{(l)}}\|u\|_{\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}}+f(w,u)\;.

We also recall the risk
\psi_{\mathcal{I}_{p},\mathcal{I}_{n}}
of the auxiliary optimization defined in
13
.

Lemma 45

(GMT on discrete sets)
.

Let
\mathcal{I}_{p}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{p}
,
\mathcal{I}_{n}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
be discrete sets, and
f
be finite on
\mathcal{I}_{p}\times\mathcal{I}_{n}
. Then for all
c\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\Psi^{\xi}_{\mathcal{I}_{p},\mathcal{I}_{n}}\geq c\big)\;\geq\;\mathbb{P}\big(\psi_{\mathcal{I}_{p},\mathcal{I}_{n}}\geq c\big)\;.

Proof of
45

Similar to the proof for the standard GMT (see e.g. proof of Lemma A.1.1 of
Thrampoulidis (2016)
), the proof relies on an application of Gordon’s Gaussian comparison inequality (see e.g. Corollary 3.13 of
Ledoux and Talagrand (1991)
) applied to two suitably defined Gaussian processes. Consider the two centred Gaussian processes indexed on the set
\mathcal{I}_{p}\times\mathcal{I}_{n}
:

\displaystyle Y_{w,u}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;w^{\intercal}\mathbf{H}u+\msum_{l=1}^{M}\xi_{l}\,\|w\|_{\Sigma^{(l)}}\|u\|_{\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}}\;,

\displaystyle X_{w,u}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\msum_{l=1}^{M}\big(\|w\|_{\Sigma^{(l)}}\mathbf{h}_{l}^{\intercal}\big(\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}\big)^{1/2}u+w^{\intercal}\big(\Sigma^{(l)}\big)^{1/2}\mathbf{g}_{l}\|u\|_{\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}}\big)\;.

To compare their second moments, we use the independence of
\mathbf{H}
and
\{\xi_{l}\}_{l\leq M}
as well as the independence of
(\mathbf{h}_{l},\mathbf{g}_{l})_{l\leq M}
: For
w,w^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{p}
and
u,u^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[Y_{w,u}Y_{w^{\prime},u^{\prime}}]-\mathbb{E}[X_{w,u}X_{w^{\prime},u^{\prime}}]

\displaystyle\;\overset{(a)}{=}\;\mathbb{E}[w^{\intercal}\mathbf{H}u(w^{\prime})^{\intercal}\mathbf{H}u^{\prime}]+\msum_{l=1}^{M}\|w\|_{\Sigma^{(l)}}\,\|w^{\prime}\|_{\Sigma^{(l)}}\,\|u\|_{\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}}\,\|u^{\prime}\|_{\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}}

\displaystyle\qquad-\msum_{l=1}^{M}\big(\|w\|_{\Sigma^{(l)}}\,\|w^{\prime}\|_{\Sigma^{(l)}}\,u^{\intercal}\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}u^{\prime}+w^{\intercal}\Sigma^{(l)}w^{\prime}\,\|u\|_{\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}}\,\|u^{\prime}\|_{\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}}\big)

\displaystyle\;\overset{(b)}{=}\;\msum_{l=1}^{M}\Big(w^{\intercal}\Sigma^{(l)}w^{\prime}\,u^{\intercal}\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}u^{\prime}+\|w\|_{\Sigma^{(l)}}\,\|w^{\prime}\|_{\Sigma^{(l)}}\,\|u\|_{\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}}\,\|u^{\prime}\|_{\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\;-\,\|w\|_{\Sigma^{(l)}}\,\|w^{\prime}\|_{\Sigma^{(l)}}\,u^{\intercal}\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}u^{\prime}\,-\,w^{\intercal}\Sigma^{(l)}w^{\prime}\|u\|_{\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}}\|u^{\prime}\|_{\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}}\Big)

\displaystyle\;=\;\msum_{l=1}^{M}\big(\|w\|_{\Sigma^{(l)}}\,\|w^{\prime}\|_{\Sigma^{(l)}}-w^{\intercal}\Sigma^{(l)}w^{\prime}\big)\big(\|u\|_{\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}}\,\|u^{\prime}\|_{\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}}-u^{\intercal}\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}u^{\prime}\big)\;.

(103)

In
(a)
, we have used that
\xi_{l}
’s,
\mathbf{h}_{l}
’s and
\mathbf{g}_{l}
’s are all standard Gaussians; in
(b)
, we have used

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[w^{\intercal}\mathbf{H}u(w^{\prime})^{\intercal}\mathbf{H}u^{\prime}]\;=

\displaystyle\;\msum_{i,i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\msum_{j,j^{\prime}=1}^{p}\,w_{i}w^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}}u_{j}u^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}}\,\mathbb{E}[H_{ij}H_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\msum_{l=1}^{M}\msum_{i,i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\msum_{j,j^{\prime}=1}^{p}\,w_{i}\Sigma^{(l)}_{ii^{\prime}}w^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}}u_{j}\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}_{jj^{\prime}}u^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\msum_{l=1}^{M}w^{\intercal}\Sigma^{(l)}w^{\prime}\,u^{\intercal}\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}u^{\prime}\;.

By the positive semi-definiteness of
\Sigma^{(l)}
and
\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}
, (
103
) is non-negative, and equals to zero when
w=w^{\prime}
. This shows that the Gaussian processes
(Y_{w,u})_{w\in\mathcal{I}_{p},u\in\mathcal{I}_{n}}
and
(X_{w,u})_{w\in\mathcal{I}_{p},u\in\mathcal{I}_{n}}
verify the conditions of the Gaussian comparison inequality (Corollary 3.13 of
Ledoux and Talagrand (1991)
) and therefore for any real sequence
(\lambda_{w,u})_{w\in\mathcal{I}_{p},u\in\mathcal{I}_{n}}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\cap_{w\in\mathcal{I}_{p}}\cup_{v\in\mathcal{I}_{n}}\,\{Y_{w,u}\geq\lambda_{w,u}\}\big)\;\geq\;\mathbb{P}\big(\cap_{w\in\mathcal{I}_{p}}\cup_{v\in\mathcal{I}_{n}}\,\{X_{w,u}\geq\lambda_{w,u}\}\big)\;.

Choosing
\lambda_{w,u}=-f(w,u)+c
yields that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\min_{w\in\mathcal{I}_{p}}\max_{v\in\mathcal{I}_{n}}\,(Y_{w,u}+f(w,u))\geq c\Big)\;\geq\;\mathbb{P}\Big(\min_{w\in\mathcal{I}_{p}}\max_{v\in\mathcal{I}_{n}}\,(X_{w,u}+f(w,u))\geq c\Big)\;.

Noting that the two min-max quantities correspond to
\Psi^{\xi}_{\mathcal{I}_{p},\mathcal{I}_{n}}
and
\psi_{\mathcal{I}_{p},\mathcal{I}_{n}}
concludes the proof.

\jmlrQED

The next result extends
45
to compact sets.

Lemma 46

(GMT for compact sets)
.

Suppose
\mathcal{S}_{w}\subset\mathbb{R}^{p}
and
\mathcal{S}_{u}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
are compact and
f
is continuous on
\mathcal{S}_{w}\times\mathcal{S}_{u}
. Then for all
c\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\Psi^{\xi}_{\mathcal{S}_{w},\mathcal{S}_{u}}\geq c)\;\geq\;\mathbb{P}(\psi_{S_{p},S_{n}}\geq c)\;.

Proof of
46

The proof is almost identical to the proof of standard GMT results for compact sets, now that we have established
45
: We show by a compactness argument that both losses only change a little when replacing
\mathcal{S}_{w}
and
\mathcal{S}_{u}
by their
\delta
-nets
\mathcal{S}^{\delta}_{p}
and
\mathcal{S}^{\delta}_{n}
, induced by the Euclidean norms on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\mathbb{R}^{d}
respectively. The only difference from their proof is that we use a slightly different concentration inequality. Therefore we only set up the essential notation, highlight the differences and refer interested readers to the proof of Theorem 3.2.1 of
Thrampoulidis (2016)
, found in Pg 185-187.

First fix some
\epsilon>0
. Since
f
is continuous and thereby uniformly continuous on the compact set
\mathcal{S}^{\delta}_{p}\times\mathcal{S}^{\delta}_{n}
, there exists some
\delta=\delta(\epsilon)>0
such that for all
(w,u),(w^{\prime},u^{\prime})\in\mathcal{S}_{w}\times\mathcal{S}_{u}
with
\|(w,u)-(w^{\prime},u^{\prime})\|\leq\delta
, we have
\|f(w,u)-f(w^{\prime},u^{\prime})\|\leq\epsilon
. Use this
\delta
to form the
\delta
-nets
\mathcal{S}^{\delta}_{p}
and
\mathcal{S}^{\delta}_{n}
. We also write
\|{\,\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}}\,}\|_{op}
as the operator norm of a matrix, and write

\displaystyle S\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\max\nolimits_{1\leq l\leq M}\max\{\|\Sigma^{(l)}\|_{\rm op}\,,\,\|\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}\|_{\rm op}\}

and

\displaystyle K\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\max\big\{\sup\nolimits_{w\in\mathcal{S}_{w}}\|w\|\,,\,\sup\nolimits_{u\in\mathcal{S}_{u}}\|u\|\big\}\;.

K
is bounded since
\mathcal{S}_{w}
and
\mathcal{S}_{u}
are compact, and for
w\in\mathcal{S}_{w}
,
u\in\mathcal{S}_{u}
and
l\leq M
, we have

\displaystyle\|w\|\,\leq\,K\,,\qquad\|w\|_{\Sigma^{(l)}}\,\leq\,SK\,,\qquad\|u\|\,\leq\,K\,,\qquad\|u\|_{\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}}\,\leq\,SK\,.

Then by the same argument as the proof of Theorem 3.2.1 of
Thrampoulidis (2016)
, there exists
w_{1}\in\mathcal{S}_{w}
,
w^{\prime}_{1}\in\mathcal{S}^{\delta}_{p}
with
\|w_{1}-w^{\prime}_{1}\|\leq\delta
and
u_{1}\in\mathcal{S}^{\delta}_{n}
such that

\displaystyle\Delta^{\xi}_{\Psi}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\min\nolimits_{w\in\mathcal{S}^{\delta}_{p}}\,\max\nolimits_{u\in\mathcal{S}^{\delta}_{n}}\,L^{\xi}_{\Psi}(w,u)\,-\,\min\nolimits_{w\in\mathcal{S}_{w}}\,\max\nolimits_{u\in\mathcal{S}_{u}}\,L^{\xi}_{\Psi}(w,u)

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;L^{\xi}_{\Psi}(w^{\prime}_{1},u_{1})-L^{\xi}_{\Psi}(w_{1},u_{1})\;.

Computing the difference gives

\displaystyle\Delta^{\xi}_{\Psi}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;(w^{\prime}_{1}-w_{1})^{\intercal}\mathbf{H}u_{1}+\msum_{l=1}^{M}\xi_{l}\,(\|w^{\prime}_{1}\|_{\Sigma^{(l)}}-\|w_{1}\|_{\Sigma^{(l)}})\|u_{1}\|_{\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}}+(f(w^{\prime}_{1},u_{1})-f(w_{1},u_{1}))

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\delta\|\mathbf{H}\|K\,+\,SK\,\msum_{l=1}^{M}|\xi_{l}|\,\|w^{\prime}_{1}-w_{1}\|_{\Sigma^{(l)}}\,+\,|f(w^{\prime}_{1},u_{1})-f(w_{1},u_{1})|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\delta K\|\mathbf{H}\|+\delta S^{2}K\msum_{l=1}^{M}|\xi_{l}|+\epsilon\;.

We seek to control
\|\mathbf{H}\|
and
\msum_{1=1}^{M}|\xi_{l}|
via concentration inequalities. Let
\text{\rm vec}(\mathbf{H})
denote the
\mathbb{R}^{pn}
-valued vector formed from the entries of
\mathbf{H}
, and
\Sigma_{\mathbf{H}}\coloneqq\text{\rm Var}[\text{\rm vec}(\mathbf{H})]
. Then we can express, for some
\mathbb{R}^{pn}
-valued standard Gaussian vector
\eta
,

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{H}\|^{2}\;=\;\|\text{\rm vec}(\mathbf{H})\|^{2}\;=\;\eta^{\intercal}\,\Sigma_{\mathbf{H}}\,\eta\;.

Then by a Chernoff bound, we have that for any
t>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\|\mathbf{H}\|\geq t)\;\leq\;\inf\nolimits_{a>0}e^{-at^{2}}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{a\|\mathbf{H}\|^{2}}\big]\;=

\displaystyle\;\inf\nolimits_{a>0}e^{-at^{2}}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{a\,\eta^{\intercal}\Sigma_{\mathbf{H}}\eta}\big]

Applying the formula of the moment-generating function of a Gaussian quadratic form (see e.g.
Rencher and Schaalje (2008)
) followed by setting
a=\frac{1}{4\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{H}}\|_{op}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\|\mathbf{H}\|\geq t)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\inf\nolimits_{a>0}\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{e^{-at^2} }{\sqrt{\textrm{det}( I_{pn} - 2 a \Sigma_\bH)}}$}}\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{e^{-t^2 / (4 \| \Sigma_\bH\|_{op} ) } }{\sqrt{\textrm{det}( I_{pn} - \frac{1}{2 \|\Sigma_\bH\|_{op} } \Sigma_\bH)}}$}}\;\leq\;2^{pn/2}\,e^{-t^{2}/(4\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{H}}\|_{op})}\;.

(104)

On the other hand, a standard concentration result on univariate Gaussians yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|\xi_{l}|>t)\;\leq\;2e^{-t^{2}/2}\;.

Taking a union bound, we obtain that for any
t>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\,\Delta^{\xi}_{\Psi}\,\leq\,\delta Kt+\delta S^{2}KMt+\epsilon\,\big)\;\geq\;1-2^{pn/2}\,e^{-t^{2}/(4\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{H}}\|_{op})}-2Me^{-t^{2}/2}\;,

and therefore for any
c\in\mathbb{R}
and
t>0
,

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}\big(\,\min\nolimits_{w\in\mathcal{S}_{w}}\,\max\nolimits_{u\in\mathcal{S}_{u}}\,L^{\xi}_{\Psi}(w,u)\,\geq\,c-\delta Kt-\delta S^{2}KMt-\epsilon\,\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\;\geq\;\mathbb{P}\big(\,\min\nolimits_{w\in\mathcal{S}^{\delta}_{p}}\,\max\nolimits_{u\in\mathcal{S}^{\delta}_{n}}\,L^{\xi}_{\Psi}(w,u)\,\geq\,c\big)-2^{pn/2}\,e^{-t^{2}/(4\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{H}}\|_{op})}-2e^{-t^{2}/2}\;.

(105)

A similar argument as in the proof of Theorem 3.2.1 of
Thrampoulidis (2016)
shows that, there exists
w_{2}\in\mathcal{S}^{\delta}_{p}
,
u_{2}\in\mathcal{S}_{w}
and
u_{2}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}^{\delta}_{n}
with
\|u_{2}-u^{\prime}_{2}\|\leq\delta
such that

\displaystyle\min_{w\in\mathcal{S}^{\delta}_{p}}\,\max_{u\in\mathcal{S}^{\delta}_{n}}\,L_{\psi}(w,u)

\displaystyle\,-\,\min_{w\in\mathcal{S}_{w}}\,\max_{u\in\mathcal{S}_{u}}\,L_{\psi}(w,u)\;\geq\;L_{\psi}(w_{2},u^{\prime}_{2})-L_{\psi}(w_{2},u_{2})

\displaystyle\;=\;\msum_{l=1}^{M}\Big(\|w_{2}\|_{\Sigma^{(l)}}\mathbf{h}_{l}^{\intercal}\big(\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}\big)^{1/2}(u^{\prime}_{2}-u_{2})+w_{2}^{\intercal}\big(\Sigma^{(l)}\big)^{1/2}\mathbf{g}_{l}(\|u^{\prime}_{2}\|_{\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}}-\|u_{2}\|_{\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}})\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad+(f(w_{2},u^{\prime}_{2})-f(w_{2},u_{2}))

\displaystyle\;\geq\;-\delta S^{2}K\msum_{l=1}^{M}(\|\mathbf{h}_{l}\|+\|\mathbf{g}_{l}\|)-\epsilon\;.

Applying (
104
) to each
\|\mathbf{h}_{l}\|
and
\|\mathbf{g}_{l}\|
yields that, for any
t>0
and
1\leq l\leq M
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\|\mathbf{h}_{l}\|\geq t)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;2^{n/2}e^{-t^{2}/4}

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\|\mathbf{g}_{l}\|\geq t)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;2^{p/2}e^{-t^{2}/4}\;.

Taking another union bound, we get that for any
t>0
,

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}(\,\min\nolimits_{w\in\mathcal{S}_{w}}\,\max\nolimits_{u\in\mathcal{S}_{u}}\,L_{\psi}(w,u)\,\geq\,c+2\delta S^{2}KMt+\epsilon\,)

\displaystyle\;\leq\;\mathbb{P}(\,\min\nolimits_{w\in\mathcal{S}_{w}^{\delta}}\,\max\nolimits_{u\in\mathcal{S}_{u}^{\delta}}\,L_{\psi}(w,u)\,\geq\,c\,)\,+\,2^{n/2}Me^{-t^{2}/4}\,+\,2^{p/2}Me^{-t^{2}/4}\;.

(106)

Now by
45
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\,\min\nolimits_{w\in\mathcal{S}_{u}^{\delta}}\,\max\nolimits_{u\in\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\delta}}\,L_{\psi}(w,u)\,\geq\,c\,)\;\leq\;\mathbb{P}\big(\,\min\nolimits_{w\in\mathcal{S}^{\delta}_{p}}\,\max\nolimits_{u\in\mathcal{S}^{\delta}_{n}}\,L^{\xi}_{\Psi}(w,u)\,\geq\,c\big)\;.

Combining this with (
105
) and (
106
) yields

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}(\,\min\nolimits_{w\in\mathcal{S}_{u}}\,\max\nolimits_{u\in\mathcal{S}_{d}}\,L_{\psi}(w,u)\,\geq\,c+2\delta S^{2}KMt+\epsilon\,)

\displaystyle\;\leq\;\mathbb{P}\big(\,\min\nolimits_{w\in\mathcal{S}_{u}}\,\max\nolimits_{u\in\mathcal{S}_{d}}\,L^{\xi}_{\Psi}(w,u)\,\geq\,c-\delta Kt-\delta S^{2}KMt-\epsilon\,\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+2^{n/2}Me^{-t^{2}/4}+2^{p/2}Me^{-t^{2}/4}+2^{np/2}\,e^{-t^{2}/(4\|\Sigma_{\mathbf{H}}\|_{op})}+2e^{-t^{2}/2}\;.

The above holds for all
\epsilon>0
and
t>0
. Set
t=\delta^{-1/2}
, take
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
and choosing a sequence
\delta(\epsilon)\rightarrow 0
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\,\min\nolimits_{w\in\mathcal{S}_{w}}\,\max\nolimits_{u\in\mathcal{S}_{u}}\,L_{\psi}(w,u)\,\geq\,c\,)\;\leq\;\mathbb{P}\big(\,\min\nolimits_{w\in\mathcal{S}_{w}}\,\max\nolimits_{u\in\mathcal{S}_{u}}\,L^{\xi}_{\Psi}(w,u)\,\geq\,c\,\big)\;,

i.e.
\mathbb{P}(\Psi^{\xi}_{\mathcal{S}_{w},\mathcal{S}_{u}}\geq c)\;\geq\;\mathbb{P}(\psi_{S_{p},S_{n}}\geq c)
.

\jmlrQED

We are now ready to prove
13
and
7
.

Proof of
13

The proof is almost identical to the proof of Theorem 3.3.1 of
Thrampoulidis (2016)
given the GMT result from
46
, and we focus on highlighting the differences. To prove the first bound in (i), we first apply
46
to obtain that for all
c\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\min_{w\in\mathcal{S}_{n}}\,\max_{u\in\mathcal{S}_{d}}\,L_{\Psi}(w,u)+\msum_{l=1}^{M}\xi_{l}\,\|w\|_{\Sigma^{(l)}}\|u\|_{\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}}\leq c\big)\;\leq\;\mathbb{P}(\psi_{S_{n},S_{d}}\leq c)\;,

where
(\xi_{l})_{l\leq M}
is a collection of univariate standard Gaussians independent of
\mathbf{H}
. First notice that, by conditioning on the event
\cap_{l\leq M}\{\xi_{l}\geq 0\}
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\Psi_{\mathcal{S}_{p},\mathcal{S}_{n}}\leq c)\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}\big(\min_{w\in\mathcal{S}_{n}}\,\max_{u\in\mathcal{S}_{d}}\,L_{\Psi}(w,u)\leq c\big)

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}\big(\min_{w\in\mathcal{S}_{n}}\,\max_{u\in\mathcal{S}_{d}}\,L_{\Psi}(w,u)+\msum_{l=1}^{M}\xi_{l}\,\|w\|_{\Sigma^{(l)}}\|u\|_{\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}}\leq c\,\big|\,\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{M}\leq 0\big)

which holds almost surely. Since
\xi_{l}
’s are all independent and symmetric about zero, and there are
2^{M}
possibilities for the signs of
(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{M})
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2^M}$}}\mathbb{P}(\Psi_{\mathcal{S}_{p},\mathcal{S}_{n}}\leq c)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2^M}$}}\mathbb{P}\big(\min_{w\in\mathcal{S}_{n}}\,\max_{u\in\mathcal{S}_{d}}\,L_{\Psi}(w,u)+\msum_{l=1}^{M}\xi_{l}\,\|w\|_{\Sigma^{(l)}}\|u\|_{\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}}\leq c\,\big|\,\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{M}\leq 0\big)

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}\big(\min_{w\in\mathcal{S}_{n}}\,\max_{u\in\mathcal{S}_{d}}\,L_{\Psi}(w,u)+\msum_{l=1}^{M}\xi_{l}\,\|w\|_{\Sigma^{(l)}}\|u\|_{\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}}\leq c\big)

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}(\psi_{S_{n},S_{d}}\leq c)\;,

which gives the desired statement.

The proof of the bound in (ii) is exactly the same as the proof of Theorem 3.3.1(ii) of
Thrampoulidis (2016)
: It relies on the ability to apply a min-max theorem or a min-max inequality for swapping minimum and maximum under the stated convex-concave assumptions, as well as the invariance of the random term of the loss under a sign change. Both hold for our losses
L_{\Psi}
and
L_{\psi}
, since
\mathbf{H}
in our
L_{\Psi}
is still zero-mean Gaussian,
L_{\psi}
is a linear sum of independent mean-zero Gaussian terms and all additional matrices
\Sigma^{(l)}
and
\tilde{\Sigma}^{(l)}
are positive semi-definite. We refer readers to the proof of Theorem 3.3.1(ii) of
Thrampoulidis (2016)
for a detailed derivation, and note that the only difference in our result is in that the coefficient from the first bound in (i) is now
2^{M}
instead of
2
.

The proof of (iii) is also exactly the same as the proof of Theorem 3.3.1(iii) of
Thrampoulidis (2016)
, which only relies on the three assumptions, the statements (i) and (ii) proved above and a union bound. We again refer readers to the proof of Theorem 3.3.1(iii) of
Thrampoulidis (2016)
for a detailed derivation.

\jmlrQED

Proof of
7

The result follows directly from
13
(ii); see Corollary 3.3.2 of
Thrampoulidis (2016)
.

\jmlrQED

Appendix M
Intermediate results for applying CGMT to data augmentation

For clarity, throughout
Appendices

M
and
N
, we will index all augmentations as
\phi_{ij}
where
i\leq m
, the number of original data, and
j\leq k
, the number of augmentations. Recall that
n=mk
. We also write the label of
\phi_{ij}(Z_{i})
as
y_{i}(Z_{i})
to emphasize the dependence on the original data
Z_{i}
.

M.1
Equivalence of different optimization problems

To prove
12
, we seek to obtain a set of deterministic equations whose solutions characterize the high-dimensional behavior of logistic regression estimate. This involves establishing the equivalence of a series of optimization problems, which are defined in this section. We also formally state all lemmas used to establish the equivalence.

Original optimization (OO).
The loss on the augmented data computed on
\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
is given as

\displaystyle L_{\beta}(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{X}^{\Phi})\;\coloneqq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\msum_{i=1}^{m}\msum_{j=1}^{k}\,\Big(\log\big( 1 + e^{( \phi_{ij} (Z_i))^\intercal\beta} \big)-y_{i}(Z_{i})\times(\phi_{ij}(Z_{i}))^{\intercal}\beta\Big)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2n}$}}\|\beta\|^{2}_{2}\;.

(107)

Here, the loss is computed on the two dependent
\mathbb{R}^{m\times p}
and
\mathbb{R}^{mk\times p}
-valued data matrices

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}\;\coloneqq\;\begin{pmatrix}\leftarrow Z_{1}^{\intercal}\rightarrow\\
\vdots\\
\leftarrow Z_{m}^{\intercal}\rightarrow\\
\end{pmatrix}\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathbf{X}^{\Phi}\;\coloneqq\;\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{X}_{1}^{\Phi}\\
\vdots\\
\mathbf{X}_{m}^{\Phi}\\
\end{pmatrix}\qquad\text{ where }\qquad\mathbf{X}^{\Phi}_{i}\;\coloneqq\;\begin{pmatrix}\leftarrow(\phi_{i1}(Z_{i}))^{\intercal}\rightarrow\\
\vdots\\
\leftarrow(\phi_{ik}(Z_{i}))^{\intercal}\rightarrow\\
\end{pmatrix}\;.

Let
S
be any convex and compact subset of
\mathbb{R}^{p}
. We denote the minimized risk over
S
and the corresponding minimizer respectively as

\displaystyle\hat{R}_{S}(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{X}^{\Phi})\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\min_{\beta\in S}L_{\beta}(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{X}^{\Phi})

and

\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{S}(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{X}^{\Phi})\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\argmin_{\beta\in S}L_{\beta}(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{X}^{\Phi})\;.

(OO)

We label (
OO
) as the
original optimization
. By our universality result, we may replace the dependent data matrices in (
OO
) by Gaussian matrices.

Gaussian optimization (GO).
Recall that
\Sigma_{o}=\text{\rm Var}[Z_{1}]
and
\Sigma=\text{\rm Var}[\phi_{11}(Z_{1})]
. We denote the corresponding minimized risk under Gaussian data as

\displaystyle\hat{R}_{S}(\mathbf{G}\Sigma_{o}^{1/2},\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}\Sigma^{1/2})\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\min\nolimits_{\beta\in S}L_{\beta}(\mathbf{G}\Sigma_{o}^{1/2},\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}\Sigma^{1/2})\;,

\displaystyle\text{ and }\qquad\hat{\beta}_{S}(\mathbf{G}\Sigma^{1/2},\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}\Sigma_{o}^{1/2})\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\argmin_{\beta\in S}L_{\beta}(\mathbf{G}\Sigma^{1/2},\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}\Sigma_{o}^{1/2})\;.

(GO)

The risk is computed on the two correlated Gaussian matrices

\displaystyle\mathbf{G}\;\coloneqq\;\begin{pmatrix}\leftarrow G_{1}^{\intercal}\rightarrow\\
\vdots\\
\leftarrow G_{m}^{\intercal}\rightarrow\\
\end{pmatrix}\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}\;\coloneqq\;\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{G}_{1}^{\Phi}\\
\vdots\\
\mathbf{G}_{m}^{\Phi}\\
\end{pmatrix}\qquad\text{ where }\qquad\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}_{i}\;\coloneqq\;\begin{pmatrix}\leftarrow(G^{\Phi}_{i1})^{\intercal}\rightarrow\\
\vdots\\
\leftarrow(G^{\Phi}_{ijk})^{\intercal}\rightarrow\\
\end{pmatrix}\;,

where
\Sigma_{o}^{1/2}G_{i}
corresponds to
Z_{i}
,
\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}_{i}\Sigma^{1/2}
corresponds to
\mathbf{X}^{\Phi}_{i}
and
\Sigma^{1/2}G^{\Phi}_{ij}
corresponds to
\phi_{ij}(Z_{i})
, and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{G}^{\Phi})]\,=

\displaystyle\,\mathbb{E}[(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{X}^{\Phi})]\,=\,0

and

\displaystyle\text{\rm Var}[(\mathbf{G}\Sigma_{o}^{1/2},\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}\Sigma^{1/2})]\,=

\displaystyle\,\mathbb{E}[(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{X}^{\Phi})]\;.

Primary optimization (PO).
Since (
GO
) only depends on Gaussian data, we may adapt the CGMT technique to analyze its limiting behaviour. This requires a reformulation of (
GO
) in a similar way to the reformulation of the primary optimization in
Salehi et al. (2019)
. To make this reformulation precise, we introduce some more notations. Given an
\mathbb{R}^{mk}
-valued vector
\bm{v}
, we denote

\displaystyle\rho(\bm{v})\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\big(\log(1 + e^{v_{11}})\,,\,\ldots\,,\,\log(1 + e^{v_{mk}})\big)^{\intercal}\;\in\;\mathbb{R}^{mk}\;.

Also write the
\mathbb{R}^{mk}
-valued vector of labels for (the Gaussian surrogates for) the augmented data as

\displaystyle\mathbf{y}(\mathbf{G}\Sigma_{o}^{1/2}\beta^{*})\;\coloneqq\;\big(\,\underbrace{y_{1}(\Sigma_{o}^{1/2}G_{1}),\ldots,y_{1}(\Sigma_{o}^{1/2}G_{1})}_{\textrm{repeated $k$ times}},\;\ldots,\;\underbrace{y_{m}(\Sigma_{o}^{1/2}G_{m}),\ldots,y_{m}(\Sigma_{o}^{1/2}G_{m})}_{\textrm{repeated $k$ times}}\,\big)^{\intercal}\;,

where we highlight that
\mathbf{y}
depends on
\mathbf{G}
only through the
\mathbb{R}^{n}
vector
\mathbf{G}\Sigma_{o}^{1/2}\beta^{*}
. For
d\in\mathbb{N}
, we also write
\mathbf{1}_{d}
as the all-one vector in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.This allows us to rewrite the loss in (
GO
) as

\displaystyle L_{\beta}(\mathbf{G}\Sigma^{1/2}_{o},\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}\Sigma^{1/2})\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{1}_{mk}^{\intercal}\,\rho(\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta)-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{y}(\mathbf{G}\Sigma_{o}^{1/2}\beta^{*})^{\intercal}\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2n}$}}\|\beta\|^{2}_{2}\;.

Introducing a new variable
u\in\mathbb{R}^{mk}
and a corresponding Lagrange multiplier
v\in\mathbb{R}^{mk}
, we can consider an alternative loss

\displaystyle L^{\rm PO}_{\beta,u,v}(\mathbf{G}\Sigma^{1/2}_{o}\beta^{*},\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}\Sigma^{1/2})\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{1}_{mk}^{\intercal}\,\rho(u)-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{y}(\mathbf{G}\Sigma^{1/2}_{o}\beta^{*})^{\intercal}u+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2n}$}}\|\beta\|^{2}_{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}v^{\intercal}(u-\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta)\;.

For subsets
S\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{p}
and
S_{u},S_{v}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{mk}
, we denote the minimized loss and the minimizer as

\displaystyle R^{\rm PO}_{S,S_{u},S_{v}}(\mathbf{G}\Sigma^{1/2}_{o}\beta^{*},\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}\Sigma^{1/2})\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\min_{\beta\in S,u\in S_{u}}\,\max_{v\in S_{v}}L^{\rm PO}_{\beta,u,v}(\mathbf{G}\Sigma^{1/2}_{o}\beta^{*},\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}\Sigma^{1/2})

\displaystyle\text{ and }\qquad\beta^{\rm PO}_{S,S_{u},S_{v}}(\mathbf{G}\Sigma^{1/2}_{o}\beta^{*},\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}\Sigma^{1/2})\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\argmin_{\beta\in S}\,\min_{u\in S_{u}}\,\max_{v\in S_{v}}L^{\rm PO}_{\beta,u,v}(\mathbf{G}\Sigma^{1/2}_{o}\beta^{*},\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}\Sigma^{1/2})\;.

(PO)

Lemma 47

(Equivalence of (
GO
) and (
PO
))
.

\displaystyle\hat{R}_{S}(\mathbf{G}\Sigma^{1/2}_{o},\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}\Sigma^{1/2})\;=

\displaystyle\;R^{\rm PO}_{S,\mathbb{R}^{mk},\mathbb{R}^{mk}}(\mathbf{G}\Sigma^{1/2}_{o}\beta^{*},\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}\Sigma^{1/2})\;,

\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{S}(\mathbf{G}\Sigma^{1/2}_{o},\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}\Sigma^{1/2})\;=

\displaystyle\;\beta^{\rm PO}_{S,\mathbb{R}^{mk},\mathbb{R}^{mk}}(\mathbf{G}\Sigma^{1/2}_{o}\beta^{*},\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}\Sigma^{1/2})\;.

Proof of
47

The proof is exactly the same to the reformulation of the primary optimization in
Salehi et al. (2019)
by the Lagrange multiplier method, and we refer readers to their (37) – (40) in Appendix C for the proof.

\jmlrQED

Auxiliary optimization (AO).
Before we present the auxiliary optimization, we notice that two key issues make our problem more complicated from the setup in
Salehi et al. (2019)
:

•

In
Salehi et al. (2019)
, they have the same data matrices for
\mathbf{G}
and
\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}
with i.i.d. standard normal entries and
\Sigma_{o}=\Sigma=I_{d}
. This allows them to project
\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}
onto the subspace orthogonal to
\beta^{*}
, which is independent of
\mathbf{G}\beta^{*}
, and apply CGMT. In our case,
\mathbf{G}
and
\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}
are different and have non-trivial dependence. We instead make use of a projection
P^{\perp}_{*}
adapted to the variance-covariance structure in
Assumption

11
, defined through

\displaystyle P_{*}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\begin{cases}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(\Sigma_* \Sigma_o^{1/2} \beta^* ) (\Sigma_* \Sigma_o^{1/2} \beta^* )^\intercal}{\| \Sigma_* \Sigma_o^{1/2} \beta^* \|^2}$}}&\text{ if }\Sigma_{*}\Sigma_{o}^{1/2}\beta^{*}\neq 0\\
0&\text{ otherwise }\;,\end{cases}

and

\displaystyle P^{\perp}_{*}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;I_{p}-P_{*}\;.

In other words,
P^{\perp}_{*}
is a projection onto the subspace orthogonal to
\Sigma_{*}\Sigma_{o}^{1/2}\beta^{*}
. This is explicitly addressed in
Section

N.1
;

•

As discussed in
Section

5
, the Gaussian matrix handled by existing work on CGMT is either one with i.i.d. coordinates, or one formed by multiplying a coordinate-wise i.i.d. matrix by an
\mathbb{R}^{m\times m}
matrix and an
\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
matrix from both sides. Our augmented matrix,
\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}
, cannot be expressed in either form due to the simultaneous presence of two forms of variances: Each row of
\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}
admits a variance of
I_{p}
, whereas the rows corresponding to different augmentations of the same data admit a variance of
\Sigma_{*}
. We resolve this issue by applying our dependent CGMT (
5
) with
M=2
.

Having addressed these two issues, we are able to borrow most of the algebraic calculations from
Salehi et al. (2019)
for analyzing the auxiliary optimization, except that the limiting terms we obtain are different due to augmentations.

To state the auxiliary optimization, let
\mathbf{g}_{1},\mathbf{g}_{2},\mathbf{h}_{1},\mathbf{h}_{2}
be independent standard Gaussians such that
\mathbf{g}_{l}
’s are
\mathbb{R}^{p}
-valued and
\mathbf{h}_{l}
’s are
\mathbb{R}^{mk}
-valued. We also denote the collections
\mathbf{g}=(\mathbf{g}_{1},\mathbf{g}_{2})
and
\mathbf{h}=(\mathbf{h}_{1},\mathbf{h}_{2})
for short, and define the matrices

\displaystyle\;\Sigma_{1}\;\coloneqq\;\Sigma^{1/2}P_{*}^{\perp}(I_{p}-\Sigma_{*})P_{*}^{\perp}\Sigma^{1/2}\;,\quad\Sigma_{2}\;\coloneqq\;\Sigma^{1/2}P_{*}^{\perp}\Sigma_{*}P_{*}^{\perp}\Sigma^{1/2}\;,\quad J_{mk}\;\coloneqq\;\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{1}_{k\times k}&&\\
&\ddots&\\
&&\mathbf{1}_{k\times k}\\
\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{mk\times mk}\;.

The loss of (
AO
), parameterized by
\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
and
u,v\in\mathbb{R}^{mk}
, is given as

\displaystyle L^{\rm AO}_{\beta,u,v}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P_{*},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{h})\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{1}_{mk}^{\intercal}\,\rho(u)-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{y}^{\intercal}u+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2n}$}}\|\beta\|^{2}_{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}v^{\intercal}(u-\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta)-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}v^{\intercal}\mathbf{h}_{1}\|\beta\|_{\Sigma_{1}}

\displaystyle\;-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\|v\|\mathbf{g}_{1}^{\intercal}\Sigma_{1}^{1/2}\beta-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk^{3/2}}$}}v^{\intercal}J_{mk}\mathbf{h}_{2}\|\beta\|_{\Sigma_{2}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\|v\|_{J_{mk}}\mathbf{g}_{2}^{\intercal}\Sigma_{2}^{1/2}\beta\;.

Note that we have abbreviated
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{y}(\mathbf{G}\Sigma_{o}^{1/2}\beta^{*})
. We also denote the minimized loss with respect to the subset
(S,S_{u},S_{v})\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{p}\times\mathbb{R}^{mk}\times\mathbb{R}^{mk}
as

\displaystyle R^{\rm AO}_{S,S_{u},S_{v}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P_{*},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{h})\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\min_{\beta\in S,u\in S_{u}}\,\max_{v\in S_{v}}L^{\rm AO}_{\beta,u,v}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P_{*},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{h})\;.

(AO)

The next result applies
5
to convert (
PO
) into (
AO
).

Lemma 48

(Equivalence of (
PO
) and (
AO
))
.

Suppose
Assumption

11
holds. Let
S\subset\mathbb{R}^{p}
and
S_{u},S_{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{mk}
be compact, convex and non-empty. Then all conclusions of
5
hold with
\Psi_{\mathcal{S}_{p},\mathcal{S}_{n}}
replaced by
R^{\rm PO}_{S,S_{u},S_{v}}(\mathbf{G}\Sigma^{1/2}_{o}\beta^{*},\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}\Sigma^{1/2})
and
\psi_{\mathcal{S}_{p},\mathcal{S}_{m}}
replaced by
R^{\rm AO}_{S,S_{u},S_{v}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P_{*},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{h})
.

Scalar optimization (SO).

The next step is to convert (
AO
) into a scalar formulation. For convenience we write
\|{\,\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}}\,}\|=\|{\,\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}}\,}\|_{2}
as the Euclidean norm throughout this section, unless otherwise specified. While the form of the optimization is complicated, we note that the terms are largely similar to the AO in
Salehi et al. (2019)
, except for additional parameters
(\sigma_{1},\nu_{1},r_{1},\tau_{1})
introduced to handle the additional covariance across different augmented versions of the same data. To define the scalar formulation, given the convex compact and non-empty subsets
S\subset\mathbb{R}^{p}
,
S_{u},S_{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{mk}
, we define the following compact domains of optimization:

\displaystyle S_{r_{1}}\;\coloneqq\;\Big\{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{mk}}$}}\|P^{\perp}_{mk}v\|\,\Big|\,v\in S_{v}\Big\}\;,\qquad S_{r_{2}}\;\coloneqq\;\Big\{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{mk}}$}}\|P_{mk}v\|\,\Big|\,v\in S_{v}\Big\}\;,

where we have defined the projection matrices
P_{mk}\coloneqq\frac{1}{k}J_{mk}
and write
P^{\perp}_{mk}=I_{mk}-P_{mk}
. Also define

\displaystyle S^{\alpha}\;\coloneqq\;\Big\{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{v(\beta^*)^\intercal\Sigma^{1/2} \beta}{\sqrt{p} \, \kappa^2_*}$}}\,\Big|\,\beta\in S\Big\}\;,

where
v(\beta^{*})\coloneqq\sqrt{p}\,\Sigma_{*}\Sigma_{o}^{1/2}\beta^{*}
,
\kappa_{*}=\|\Sigma_{*}\Sigma_{o}^{1/2}\beta^{*}\|
. Define

\displaystyle S_{\sigma_{1}}\;\coloneqq\;\Big\{\|(I_{p}-\Sigma_{*})P_{*}^{\perp}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta\|\,\big|\,\beta\in S\Big\}\;,\qquad S_{\sigma_{2}}\;\coloneqq\;\Big\{\|\Sigma_{*}P_{*}^{\perp}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta\|\,\big|\,\beta\in S\Big\}\;.

The optimization will be performed over the two
\mathbb{R}^{5}
-valued vectors

\displaystyle\tilde{\alpha}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;(\alpha,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\nu_{1},\nu_{2})\;\in\;S^{\alpha}\times S_{\sigma_{1}}\times S_{\sigma_{2}}\times(\mathbb{R}^{+}_{0})^{2}\;\coloneqq\;S_{1}\;,

\displaystyle\tilde{\theta}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;(r_{1},r_{2},\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\theta)\;\in\;S_{r_{1}}\times S_{r_{2}}\times(\mathbb{R}^{+}_{0})^{2}\times\mathbb{R}\;\coloneqq\;S_{2}\;.

We also define
P_{\Sigma}=(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}\Sigma^{1/2}
, the projection onto the positive eigenspace of
\Sigma
, and the matrix

\displaystyle\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2 \sigma_1 \tau_1}$}}(P_{\Sigma}-\Sigma_{*})+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2 \sigma_2 \tau_2}$}}\Sigma_{*}\;.

Also define the Gaussian random vectors

\displaystyle\;\mathbf{q}\;\coloneqq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\kappa_* \, \sqrt{p}}$}}\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}\,v(\beta^{*})\;=\;\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\Sigma_* \Sigma_o^{1/2} \beta^*}{\| \Sigma_* \Sigma_o^{1/2} \beta^* \|}$}}\;,\qquad\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma}\;\coloneqq\;\kappa_{*}\alpha\mathbf{q}-\sigma_{1}\mathbf{h}_{1}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_2}{\sqrt{k}}$}}J_{mk}\mathbf{h}_{2}\;,

\displaystyle\;\tilde{\mathbf{g}}\;\coloneqq\;-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_1 + r_2}{\sqrt{mk}}$}}\,(P_{\Sigma}-\Sigma_{*})\mathbf{g}_{1}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2}{\sqrt{m}}$}}\,\Sigma_{*}\mathbf{g}_{2}\;.

For a function
f:\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
and some
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{mk}
, we define the Moreau envelope

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{S}(f;v,t)\;\coloneqq\;\min_{x\in\mathcal{S}}f(x)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2 t}$}}\|x-v\|^{2}_{2}\;.

Now we are ready to define the loss

\displaystyle L^{\rm SO}_{\tilde{\alpha},\tilde{\theta}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{h})\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_1}{2 \tau_1}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_2}{2 \tau_2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_1}{2 \nu_1}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2}{2 \nu_2}$}}+\alpha\theta\kappa_{*}^{2}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\alpha^2 \kappa_*^2}{2 \sigma_2 \tau_2}$}}+M_{\mathbf{g},\sigma,\tau,\theta}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{4}$}}\big\|(\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}^{\dagger})^{1/2}\big(\tilde{\mathbf{g}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta}{\sqrt{p}}$}}v(\beta^{*})\big)\big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\,+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}M_{\mathbf{y},\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma},r,\nu}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2 r_2 \nu_2 mk }$}}\|\mathbf{y}\|^{2}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{y}^{\intercal}\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma}\;,

where we have defined the nested Moreau envelope
M_{\mathbf{y},\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma},r,\nu}
via

\displaystyle M^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma},r,\nu}(\tilde{u})\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathcal{M}_{P_{mk}^{\perp}(S_{u})}\big(\mathbf{1}_{mk}^{\intercal}\,\rho(\tilde{u}+{\,\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}}\,})\,;\,P^{\perp}_{mk}\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma},\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{r_1 \nu_1}$}}\big)\;,

\displaystyle M_{\mathbf{y},\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma},r,\nu}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathcal{M}_{P_{mk}(S_{u})}\big(M^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma},r,\nu}\,;\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{r_2 \nu_2}$}}\mathbf{y}-P_{mk}\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma}\,,\,r_{2}\nu_{2}\big)\;,

as well as another Moreau envelope like term

\displaystyle M_{\mathbf{g},\sigma,\tau,\theta}\;\coloneqq\;\min_{\mu\in S}\,

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2n}$}}\|P_{\Sigma}\mu\|^{2}_{2}+\big\|\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}^{1/2}(\Sigma^{1/2}\mu)-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}(\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}^{\dagger})^{1/2}\big(\tilde{\mathbf{g}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta}{\sqrt{p}}$}}v(\beta^{*})\big)\big\|^{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2}{\sqrt{n}}$}}\mathbf{g}_{2}^{\intercal}P_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}\mu\;.

The minimized risk is denoted as

\displaystyle R^{\rm SO}_{S,S_{u},S_{v}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{h})\;\coloneqq\;\min_{\tilde{\alpha}\in S_{1}}\,\max_{\tilde{\theta}\in S_{2}}\,\min_{\tilde{\chi}\in S_{3}}L^{\rm SO}_{\tilde{\alpha}\tilde{\theta}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{h})\;.

(SO)

The next lemma shows that (
AO
) can be replaced by (
SO
) in that it satisfies similar inequalities as (
AO
) in terms of their relationships to (
PO
). The inequalities in the result are to be compared with those in
5
.

Lemma 49

(Equivalence of (
PO
) and (
SO
))
.

Let
S\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
and
S_{u},S_{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{mk}
be compact, convex and non-empty. Also assume that the linear span
\textrm{span}(S)=\mathbb{R}^{p}
. Then for any
c\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(R^{\rm PO}_{S,S_{u},S_{v}}\leq c)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;4\,\mathbb{P}(R^{\rm SO}_{S,S_{u},S_{v}}\,\leq\,c)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(R^{\rm PO}_{S,S_{u},S_{v}}\geq c)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;4\,\mathbb{P}(R^{\rm SO}_{S,S_{u},S_{v}}\,\geq\,c)\;.

If instead of
S
, the set of values of
\beta
we consider is the non-convex set

\displaystyle S_{c,\epsilon}\;\coloneqq\;S\setminus\big\{\beta\in S\;\big|\;|(P_{\Sigma}\beta)^{\intercal}\Sigma_{o}(P_{\Sigma}\beta)-c|\leq\epsilon\;\big\}

for some
c\in\mathbb{R}
and some sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
such that
S_{c,\epsilon}
is non-empty. Then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(R^{\rm PO}_{S_{c,\epsilon},S_{u},S_{v}}\leq c)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;4\,\mathbb{P}(R^{\rm SO}_{S_{c,\epsilon},S_{u},S_{v}}\,\leq\,c)\;.

Deterministic optimization (DO).
The next step is to compute the asymptotics of (
SO
) as
m,p\rightarrow\infty
and
p/m\rightarrow\kappa/k
for special cases of
S\subset\mathbb{R}^{p}
. The limit is given by a deterministic optimization

\displaystyle R^{DO}_{S}\;\coloneqq\;\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in S^{\alpha}\\
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})\in S_{\sigma_{1}}\times S_{\sigma_{2}}\\
\nu_{1},\nu_{2}\geq 0\end{subarray}}\,\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}(r_{1},r_{2})\in S_{r_{1}}\times S_{r_{2}}\\
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\geq 0\\
\theta\in\mathbb{R}\end{subarray}}\,

\displaystyle\,-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_1}{2 \tau_1}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_2}{2 \tau_2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_1}{2 \nu_1}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2}{2 \nu_2}$}}+\alpha\theta\bar{\kappa}_{*}^{2}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\alpha^2 \bar\kappa_*^2}{2 \sigma_2 \tau_2}$}}-\bar{\chi}^{r,\theta,\sigma,\tau}_{1}+\epsilon_{S}^{2}\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\bar\chi^{r, \theta, \sigma, \tau}_3}{\bar\chi^{r, \theta, \sigma, \tau}_2}$}}

\displaystyle\,-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{4 r_2 \nu_2}$}}-\alpha\,\mathbb{E}\big[\sigma(\bar{\kappa}_{o}\bar{Z}_{0}+\bar{\kappa}_{*}\bar{Z}_{1})\bar{\kappa}_{*}\bar{Z}_{1}\big]+\bar{M}_{\rho}^{r,\nu,\alpha,\sigma}\;,

(DO)

where we have defined the limits

\displaystyle\bar{\kappa}_{*}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\lim_{p\rightarrow\infty}\kappa_{*}\;=\;\lim_{p\rightarrow\infty}\|\Sigma_{*}\Sigma_{o}^{1/2}\beta^{*}\|\;,\qquad\bar{\kappa}_{o}\;\coloneqq\;\lim_{p\rightarrow\infty}\|(I_{p}-\Sigma_{*})\Sigma_{o}^{1/2}\beta^{*}\|\;,

\displaystyle\bar{\chi}^{r,\theta,\sigma,\tau}_{1}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(r_1 + r_2)^2 \sigma_1 \tau_1}{2k}$}}\,\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{11}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2^2 \sigma_2 \tau_2}{2}$}}\,\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{12}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta^2 \bar\kappa_*^2 \sigma_2 \tau_2}{2}$}}\,\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{13}\;,

\displaystyle\bar{\chi}^{r,\theta,\sigma,\tau}_{2}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(r_1+r_2)^2\sigma_1^2\tau_1^2}{k}$}}\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{21}+r_{2}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{2}\tau_{2}^{2}\,\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{22}+\theta^{2}\bar{\kappa}_{*}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{2}\tau_{2}^{2}\,\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{23}\;,

\displaystyle\bar{\chi}^{r,\theta,\sigma,\tau}_{3}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(r_1+r_2)^2\sigma_1^2\tau_1^2}{k}$}}\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{31}+r_{2}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{2}\tau_{2}^{2}\,\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{32}+\theta^{2}\bar{\kappa}_{*}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{2}\tau_{2}^{2}\,\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{33}\;,

with

\displaystyle\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{11}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\lim\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\Tr\big(
\big( \frac{\sigma_1 \tau_1\lambda}{m} \Sigma^\dagger+
I_p \big)^\dagger(P_\Sigma- \Sigma_*)
\big)
}{m}$}}\;,

\displaystyle\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{12}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\lim\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\Tr\big(
\big(
\frac{\sigma_2 \tau_2\lambda}{m} \Sigma^\dagger+
I_p
\big)^\dagger\Sigma_*
\big)
}{m}$}}\;,

\displaystyle\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{13}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\lim\Tr\Big(
\Big(
\mfrac{\sigma_2 \tau_2\lambda}{m} \Sigma^\dagger+
I_p
\Big)^\dagger P_*
\Big)\;,

\displaystyle\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{21}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\lim\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\big\|
\Sigma_{\rm new}^{1/2} (\Sigma^\dagger)^{1/2}
\big( \frac{\sigma_1 \tau_1 \lambda}{m} \Sigma^\dagger+ I_p \big)^\dagger(P_\Sigma- \Sigma_*)
\big\|^2
}{m}$}}\;,

\displaystyle\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{22}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\lim\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\big\|
\Sigma_{\rm new}^{1/2} (\Sigma^\dagger)^{1/2}
\Big( \mfrac{\sigma_2 \tau_2 \lambda}{m} \Sigma^\dagger+ I_p \Big)^\dagger\Sigma_*
\big\|^2
}{m}$}}\;,

\displaystyle\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{23}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\lim\,\Big\|\Sigma_{\rm new}^{1/2}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_2 \tau_2 \lambda}{m}$}}\Sigma^{\dagger}+I_{p}\Big)^{\dagger}P_{*}\Big\|^{2}\;,

\displaystyle\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{31}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\lim\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\big\|
\big( \frac{\lambda}{2m} \Sigma^\dagger+ \tilde\Sigma_{\sigma, \tau}
\big)^{1/2}
\Sigma^{1/2} P_{\Sigma_{\rm new}}
(\Sigma^\dagger)^{1/2}
\big( \frac{\sigma_1 \tau_1 \lambda}{m} \Sigma^\dagger+ I_p \big)^\dagger(P_\Sigma- \Sigma_*)
\big\|^2
}{m}$}}\;,

\displaystyle\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{32}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\lim\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\big\|
\big( \frac{\lambda}{2m} \Sigma^\dagger+ \tilde\Sigma_{\sigma, \tau}
\big)^{1/2}
\Sigma^{1/2} P_{\Sigma_{\rm new}}
(\Sigma^\dagger)^{1/2}
\Big( \mfrac{\sigma_2 \tau_2 \lambda}{m} \Sigma^\dagger+ I_p \Big)^\dagger\Sigma_*
\big\|^2
}{m}$}}\;,

\displaystyle\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{33}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\lim\,\Big\|\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2m}$}}\Sigma^{\dagger}+\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}\Big)^{1/2}\Sigma^{1/2}P_{\Sigma_{\rm new}}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_2 \tau_2 \lambda}{m}$}}\Sigma^{\dagger}+I_{p}\Big)^{\dagger}P_{*}\Big\|^{2}\;.

We have also defined an expected Moreau-envelope-like term

\displaystyle\bar{M}_{\rho}^{r,\nu,\alpha,\sigma}\;\coloneqq\;

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\bigg[\min_{\tilde{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{k}$}}\mathbf{1}_{k}^{\intercal}\rho(\tilde{u})+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_1 \nu_1}{2 k}$}}\,\Big\|\big(I_{k}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{k}$}}\mathbf{1}_{k\times k}\big)(\tilde{u}+\sigma_{1}\eta)\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2 \nu_2}{2 k}$}}\Big\|\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{k}$}}\mathbf{1}_{k\times k}\Big(\tilde{u}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{r_2 \nu_2}$}}\mathbb{I}_{\geq 0}\{\bar{\kappa}_{o}\bar{Z}_{0}+\bar{\kappa}_{*}\bar{Z}_{1}-\varepsilon_{1}\}\mathbf{1}_{k}-\alpha\bar{\kappa}_{*}\bar{Z}_{1}\mathbf{1}_{k}+\sigma_{1}\eta+\sigma_{2}\bar{Z}_{2}\mathbf{1}_{k}\Big)\Big\|^{2}\bigg]\;,

where
\bar{Z}_{0},\bar{Z}_{1},\bar{Z}_{2},\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{k}
are i.i.d. univariate Gaussians and
\eta=(\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{k})
, and
\varepsilon_{1}
is an independent
\textrm{Logistic}(0,1)
variable. The two cases of
S
we consider are

\displaystyle S\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathcal{S}_{p}

and

\displaystyle S\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathcal{S}_{\epsilon}^{c}\;\coloneqq\;\big\{\beta\in\mathcal{S}_{p}\,\big|\,\big|\sqrt{\beta^{\intercal}\Sigma_{\rm new}\beta}-(\bar{\chi}^{\bar{r},\bar{\theta},\bar{\sigma},\bar{\tau}}_{2})^{1/2}\big|>\epsilon\big\}\;,

where
\bar{r}=(\bar{r}_{1},\bar{r}_{2})
,
\bar{\sigma}=(\bar{\sigma}_{1},\bar{\sigma}_{2})
,
\bar{\theta}
and
\bar{\tau}=(\bar{\tau}_{1},\bar{\tau}_{2})
are the optimal solutions to (
DO
). We also set
\epsilon_{S}=0
for
S=\mathcal{S}_{p}
and
\epsilon_{S}=\epsilon
for
S=\mathcal{S}_{\epsilon}^{c}
.

Lemma 50

(Equivalence between (
SO
) and (
DO
))
.

Assume that the set
S_{u}\subset\mathbb{R}^{mk}
is closed under permutation of the
m
blocks of
k
coordinates. Also suppose that as
m,p\rightarrow\infty
,

\sup_{u\in S_{u}}\frac{\|u\|_{2}^{2}}{mk}\rightarrow\infty
and
\sup_{u\in S_{v}}\frac{\|v\|_{2}^{2}}{mk}\rightarrow\infty
. Also assume that the limits
\bar{\kappa}_{*}
,
\bar{\chi}^{r,\theta,\sigma,\tau}_{1}
,
\bar{\chi}^{r,\theta,\sigma,\tau}_{2}
and
\bar{\chi}^{r,\theta,\sigma,\tau}_{3}
exist for every
r_{1},r_{2},\theta,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\tau_{1},\tau_{2}
. Then for
S=\mathcal{S}_{p}
and
S=\mathcal{S}_{\epsilon}^{c}
,

\displaystyle\big|\,R^{\rm SO}_{S,S_{u},S_{v}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{h})-R^{\rm DO}_{S}\,\big|\;\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}\;0\;.

As with
Salehi et al. (2019)
, it remains to prove that the first order condition of (
DO
) for
S=\mathcal{S}_{p}
is equivalent to the system of
10
equations (
EQs
) in
(\alpha,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\nu_{1},\nu_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\theta)
. This involves computing the derivative of the Moreau-envelope-like term
\bar{M}^{r,\nu,\alpha,\sigma}_{\rho}
using the envelope theorem.

Lemma 51
.

Assume that the minimizer-maximizers of (
DO
) are within the interior of the domain of optimization and that
S=\mathcal{S}_{p}
. Then these minimizer-maximizers are solutions to (
EQs
).

M.2
Verifying conditions for different augmnetations

Isotropic data with no augmentation.

Salehi et al. (2019)
derives a set of equations that governs the behavior of high-dimensional logistic regression with ridge regularization, isotropic data and no data augmentation. Here, we verify that our formula recover their formula exactly as a special case, and that
(r_{2},\nu_{2},\sigma_{2},\tau_{2},\alpha,\theta)
play the role of the parameters in the original unaugmented optimization.

Lemma 52
.

Suppose that
X_{\rm new}\overset{d}{=}Z_{1}
with
\text{\rm Var}[Z_{1}]=\frac{1}{p}I_{p}
, that
k=1
and
\phi_{1}(Z_{i})=Z_{i}
almost surely for all
i\leq m=n
. Also write
\gamma=\frac{1}{r_{2}\nu_{2}}
,
\rho({\,\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}}\,})=\log(1+\exp(\argdot))
and denote the proximal operator
{\rm Prox}_{t\rho({\,\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}}\,})}(v)\coloneqq\argmin_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{2t}(v-x)^{2}+\rho(x)
. Then (
EQs
) is equivalent to the following system of equations:

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\theta\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\alpha\kappa}{\gamma}$}}\;,\\
\tau_{2}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \kappa^{-1} \gamma}{\sigma_2(1 - \gamma\lambda)}$}}\;,\\
r_{2}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_2 \sqrt{ \kappa}}{\gamma}$}}\;,\\
\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma^2 \kappa}{2}$}}\;=\;\mathbb{E}\big[\partial\rho(-\bar{\kappa}_{*}\bar{Z}_{1})\big(\alpha\bar{\kappa}_{*}\bar{Z}_{1}+\sigma_{2}\bar{Z}_{2}-{\rm Prox}_{\gamma\rho({\,\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}}\,})}(\alpha\bar{\kappa}_{*}\bar{Z}_{1}+\sigma_{2}\bar{Z}_{2})\big)^{2}\big]\;,\\
1-\kappa+\gamma\lambda\kappa\;=\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ 2 \rho'(- \bar\kappa_* Z_1)}{1 + \gamma\rho''({\rm Prox}_{\gamma\rho(\argdot)} ( \bar\kappa_* \alpha\bar Z_1 + \sigma_2 \bar Z_2 ) )}$}}\Big]\;,\\
