-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\alpha\kappa}{2}$}}\;=\;\mathbb{E}[\partial^{2}\rho(-\bar{\kappa}_{*}\bar{Z}_{1}){\rm Prox}_{\gamma\rho({\,\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}}\,})}(\bar{\kappa}_{*}\alpha\bar{Z}_{1}+\sigma_{2}\bar{Z}_{2})]\;,\end{cases}

with
r_{1}=\sigma_{1}=0
,
\nu_{1},\tau_{1}\rightarrow\infty
,
\bar{\kappa}_{*}=\lim_{p\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\|\beta^{*}\|}{\sqrt{p}}
and
\kappa=\lim p/n
.

Remark 53
.

(i)
51
and
12
apply even though the values of
r_{1}
,
\sigma_{1}
,
\nu_{1}
and
\tau_{1}
are not in the interior of the domain of optimization, as these variables can be removed much earlier in the proof of
49
and allow us to handle only a smaller system of equations. Moreover, the only quantity
\bar{\chi}^{r,\theta,\sigma,\tau}_{2}
that enters the test risk is independent of these variables.
(ii) To identify the equations in
52
with those in (14) and (16) from Theorem 2 of
Salehi et al. (2019)
, we note several notational differences: We have used
\kappa=\lim p/n
, whereas they use
\delta=\lim n/p
; our
\bar{\kappa}_{*}
,
\bar{Z}_{1}
and
\bar{Z}_{2}
should be identified with their
\kappa
,
Z_{1}
and
Z_{2}
; our
r_{2}
,
\sigma_{2}
and
\tau_{2}
should be identified with their
r
,
\sigma
and
\tau
; our regularization is defined as
\frac{\lambda}{2n}\|{\,\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}}\,}\|^{2}
whereas theirs is defined as
\frac{\lambda}{2p}\|{\,\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}}\,}\|^{2}
, so to see the equivalence, one needs to make the replacement
\lambda\mapsto\lambda\kappa^{-1}
above.

Random permutations and sign flipping.
Recall the setup for random permutations and random sign flipping in
Section

6
. We first verify that the equations (
EQs
) do apply to these two augmentations in special cases. In view of
Theorem

12
, the key condition to verify is
Assumption

11
.

Lemma 54
.

Suppose the coordinates of each
Z_{1}^{(t)}
are i.i.d. within the group. Then
Assumption

11
holds for random permutations.

Lemma 55
.

Suppose
\text{\rm Var}[Z_{1}]=\frac{1}{p}I_{p}
. Then
Assumption

11
holds for random sign flipping.

Random cropping.
Recall the random cropping scheme defined in
Section

6
. While random cropping does not satisfy
Assumption

11
, it does satisfy a slightly relaxed notion of
Assumption

11
:

Lemma 56
.

Suppose
\text{\rm Var}[Z_{1}]=\frac{1}{p}I_{p}
. For random cropping, there exist some
a_{1},a_{2}>0
such that

\displaystyle(i)\;\Sigma_{*}\;=

\displaystyle\;a_{1}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}\,\text{\rm Cov}[\phi_{1}(Z_{1})\,,\,Z_{1}](\Sigma_{o}^{\dagger})^{1/2}

and

\displaystyle(ii)\;\Sigma_{*}^{2}\;=

\displaystyle\;a_{2}\Sigma_{*}\;.

(108)

The core CGMT statement — the equivalence of (
PO
) and (
AO
) — does hold for random cropping. To see this, notice that
Assumption

11
is equivalent to having
a_{1}=a_{2}=1
in (
108
). We observe that to prove the equivalence of (
PO
) and (
AO
) in
48
,
Assumption

11
is only critical for showing the independence of the differently projected data matrices, which hold even under the rescaling
a_{1}
and
a_{2}
in (
108
); see the proof of
57
below. As such,

Meanwhile, a tedious extension of (
EQs
) also holds for random cropping. Notice that
Assumption

11
is used again only in the calculations from (
110
) onwards in the proof of
49
, which relates (
AO
) to (
SO
). There, we only use the idempotency of
\Sigma_{*}
such that
\Sigma_{*}
and
I_{p}-\Sigma_{*}
are projections onto orthogonal subspaces, which simplify many subsequent calculations. If instead (
108
)(ii) holds, a similar calculation still works by writing
\Sigma_{*}=\Sigma_{1}+\Sigma_{2}
and
I_{p}-\Sigma_{*}=\Sigma^{\prime}_{2}+\Sigma_{3}
, such that
\Sigma_{1}
,
\Sigma_{2}
and
\Sigma_{3}
have mutually orthogonal positive eigenspaces, and
\Sigma_{2}
and
\Sigma^{\prime}_{2}
share the same positive eigenspace. This would lead to a system of equations involving
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{3})
instead of just
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\tau_{1},\tau_{2})
in (
EQs
), and we omit the calculations for simplicity.

Appendix N
Proofs for
Appendix

M

N.1
Proofs for the equivalence of (
PO
) and (
AO
)

The next lemma confirms that the projection
P_{*}
decouples the different random quantities.

Lemma 57
.

Under
Assumption

11
,
\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P^{\perp}_{*}
is independent of
(\mathbf{G}\Sigma^{1/2}_{o}\beta^{*},\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P_{*})
.

Proof of
57

By Gaussianity, to prove independence, it suffices to check that the covariance between the random quantities are zero. We first verify that the covariance between
\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P^{\perp}_{*}
and
\mathbf{G}\Sigma^{1/2}_{o}\beta^{*}
is zero, for which it suffices to compute

\displaystyle\text{\rm Cov}[P^{\perp}_{*}G^{\Phi}_{11}\,,\,G_{1}^{\perp}\Sigma^{1/2}_{o}\beta^{*}]\;=

\displaystyle\;P^{\perp}_{*}\,\text{\rm Cov}[(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}\phi_{11}(X_{1}),(\Sigma_{o}^{\dagger})^{1/2}X_{1}]\,\Sigma^{1/2}_{o}\beta^{*}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;P^{\perp}_{*}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}\,\text{\rm Cov}[\phi_{11}(X_{1}),X_{1}]\,(\Sigma_{o}^{\dagger})^{1/2}\Sigma^{1/2}_{o}\beta^{*}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;P^{\perp}_{*}\Sigma_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}_{o}\beta^{*}\;=\;0\;.

In the last line, we used
Assumption

11
(i),
and concluded that the covariance evaluates to zero by the definition of
P_{*}
. This proves that
\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P^{\perp}_{*}
is independent of
\mathbf{G}\Sigma^{1/2}_{o}\beta^{*}
.

To check the independence between
\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P^{\perp}_{*}
and
\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P_{*}
, we first note that since
\Sigma=\text{\rm Var}[\phi_{11}(X_{1})]
, we have

\displaystyle\text{\rm Cov}[P^{\perp}_{*}G^{\Phi}_{11}\,,\,P_{*}G^{\Phi}_{11}]\;=

\displaystyle\;P^{\perp}_{*}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}\,\text{\rm Var}[\phi_{11}(X_{1})]\,(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}P_{*}\;=\;P^{\perp}_{*}P_{*}\;=\;0\;.

We also need to compute

\displaystyle\text{\rm Cov}[P^{\perp}_{*}G^{\Phi}_{11}\,,\,P_{*}G^{\Phi}_{12}]\;=

\displaystyle\;P^{\perp}_{*}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}\,\text{\rm Cov}[\phi_{11}(X_{1})\,,\,\phi_{12}(X_{1})]\,(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}P_{*}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;P^{\perp}_{*}\,\Sigma_{*}\,P_{*}\;.

Now note that if
\Sigma_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}_{o}\beta^{*}=0
, the above evaluates to zero automatically. Otherwise, we have

\displaystyle\Sigma_{*}\,P_{*}\;=

\displaystyle\;\Sigma_{*}\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(\Sigma_* \Sigma_o^{1/2} \beta^* ) (\Sigma_* \Sigma_o^{1/2} \beta^* )^\intercal}{\| \Sigma_* \Sigma_o^{1/2} \beta^* \|^2}$}}\;=\;P_{*}\;,

where we have used
\Sigma_{*}^{2}=\Sigma_{*}
by
Assumption

11
(ii). This implies

\displaystyle\text{\rm Cov}[P^{\perp}_{*}G^{\Phi}_{11}\,,\,P_{*}G^{\Phi}_{12}]\;=\;P^{\perp}_{*}P_{*}\;=\;0\;,

which proves that
\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P^{\perp}_{*}
is independent of
\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P_{*}
.

\jmlrQED

57
suggests that we can apply
5
to
\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P_{*}^{\perp}\Sigma^{1/2}
conditionally on
(\mathbf{G}\Sigma^{1/2}_{o}\beta^{*},\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P_{*})
. To facilitate this, the next lemma computes the covariance structure of
\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P_{*}^{\perp}\Sigma^{1/2}
.

Lemma 58
.

For
i,i^{\prime}\leq m
,
j,j^{\prime}\leq k
and
l,l^{\prime}\leq p
,

\displaystyle\text{\rm Cov}[(\Sigma^{1/2}P_{*}^{\perp}G^{\Phi}_{ij})_{l}\,,\,(\Sigma^{1/2}P_{*}^{\perp}G^{\Phi}_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}})_{l^{\prime}}]\;=\;(I_{mk})_{ij,i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}\,(\Sigma_{1})_{l,l^{\prime}}+(J_{mk})_{ij,i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}\,(\Sigma_{2})_{l,l^{\prime}}\;.

Moreover,
\Sigma_{*}
,
\Sigma_{1}
and
\Sigma_{2}
are all positive semi-definite.

Proof of
58

For
i,i^{\prime}\leq m
,
j,j^{\prime}\leq k
and
l,l^{\prime}\leq p
, we have

\displaystyle\;\text{\rm Cov}[(\Sigma^{1/2}P_{*}^{\perp}G^{\Phi}_{ij})_{l}\,,\,(\Sigma^{1/2}P_{*}^{\perp}G^{\Phi}_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}})_{l^{\prime}}]

\displaystyle\;=\;\text{\rm Cov}[(\Sigma^{1/2}P_{*}^{\perp}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}\phi_{ij}(X_{i}))_{l}\,,\,(\Sigma^{1/2}P_{*}^{\perp}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}\phi_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}(X_{i^{\prime}}))_{l^{\prime}}]

\displaystyle\;=\;\mathbb{I}\{i=i^{\prime}\}\mathbb{I}\{j=j^{\prime}\}\,\big(\,\Sigma^{1/2}P^{\perp}_{*}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}\,\text{\rm Var}[\phi_{11}(X_{1})]\,(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}P^{\perp}_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}\,\big)_{l,l^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{I}\{i=i^{\prime}\}\mathbb{I}\{j\neq j^{\prime}\}\,\big(\,\Sigma^{1/2}P^{\perp}_{*}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}\,\text{\rm Cov}[\phi_{11}(X_{1})\,,\,\phi_{12}(X_{1})]\,(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}P^{\perp}_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}\,\big)_{l,l^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\;\overset{(a)}{=}\;(I_{mk})_{ij,i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}\,\big(\Sigma^{1/2}P^{\perp}_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}-\Sigma^{1/2}P^{\perp}_{*}\Sigma_{*}P^{\perp}_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}\big)_{l,l^{\prime}}+(J_{mk})_{ij,i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}\,\big(\Sigma^{1/2}P^{\perp}_{*}\Sigma_{*}P^{\perp}_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}\big)_{l,l^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\;=\;(I_{mk})_{ij,i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}\,(\Sigma_{1})_{l,l^{\prime}}+(J_{mk})_{ij,i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}\,(\Sigma_{2})_{l,l^{\prime}}\;.

In
(a)
, we have used that
(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}\,\text{\rm Var}[\phi_{11}(X_{1})]\,(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}=I_{p}
,
(P^{\perp}_{*})^{2}=P^{\perp}_{*}
and the definition of
\Sigma_{*}
. This gives the desired formula. Now by the total law of covariance (see e.g. Lemma 41(i) of
Huang et al. (2022)
),

\displaystyle\Sigma_{*}\;=

\displaystyle\;(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}\,\text{\rm Cov}[\phi_{11}(X_{1})\,,\,P^{\perp}_{*}\phi_{12}(X_{1})]\,(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}\,\text{\rm Var}\,\mathbb{E}[\phi_{11}(X_{1})\,|\,X_{1}]\,(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}

which is positive semi-definite. This implies that
\Sigma_{2}
is also positive semi-definite. Moreover, by another total law of variance, we get that

\displaystyle\Sigma_{*}\;\preceq\;(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}\,\text{\rm Var}[\phi_{11}(X_{1})]\,(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}\;=\;I_{p}\;,

where
\preceq
denotes the Loewner partial order on positive semi-definite matrices. This implies that
I_{p}-\Sigma_{*}
is positive semi-definite and so is
\Sigma_{1}
.

\jmlrQED

We are now ready to prove the equivalence of (
PO
) and (
AO
).

Proof of
48

We recall that (
PO
) can be expressed as

\displaystyle\min_{\beta\in S,u\in S_{u}}\,\max_{v\in S_{v}}\;

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{1}_{nk}^{\intercal}\,\rho(u)-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{y}(\mathbf{G}\Sigma^{1/2}_{o}\beta^{*})^{\intercal}u+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2m}$}}\|\beta\|^{2}_{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}v^{\intercal}u

\displaystyle\;-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}v^{\intercal}\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}v^{\intercal}\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P^{\perp}_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta\;.

By
57
,
\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P^{\perp}_{*}
is independent of
(\mathbf{G}\Sigma^{1/2}_{o}\beta^{*},\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P_{*})
. This allows us to condition on the random variables
(\mathbf{G}\Sigma^{1/2}_{o}\beta^{*},\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P_{*})
, apply the CGMT result to
\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P^{\perp}_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}
, and then marginalize out
(\mathbf{G}\Sigma^{1/2}_{o}\beta^{*},\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P_{*})
. Notice that the loss is convex-concave in
(\beta,v)
, the sets of optimization are compact convex, and the variance-covariance structure of
\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P^{\perp}_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}
is given by
58
, which satisfies the condition of
5
with
M=2
. The conclusions of
5
therefore hold for (
PO
) and (
AO
).

\jmlrQED

N.2
Proof of
49
: Equivalence between (
PO
), (
AO
) and (
SO
)

The calculations are mostly similar to that of
Salehi et al. (2019)
, so we focus on highlighting the differences in the proof.

Analyzing the auxiliary optimization.
We first notice that, other than the regularization term
\|\beta\|^{2}_{2}
,
\beta
appears in the loss only through
\Sigma^{1/2}\beta
,
\Sigma_{1}^{1/2}\beta
and
\Sigma_{2}^{1/2}\beta
, where

\displaystyle\Sigma_{1}\;=

\displaystyle\;\Sigma^{1/2}P_{*}^{\perp}(I_{p}-\Sigma_{*})P_{*}^{\perp}\Sigma^{1/2}

and

\displaystyle\Sigma_{2}\;=

\displaystyle\;\Sigma^{1/2}P_{*}^{\perp}\Sigma^{1/2}\;.

Therefore it suffices to restrict the set of minimization,
\beta\in S
, to the intersection of
S
and the positive eigenspace of
\Sigma
. Define the projection to the positive eigenspace of
\Sigma
as
P_{\Sigma}\coloneqq\Sigma^{\dagger}\Sigma
, which allows us to rewrite the auxiliary optimization as

\displaystyle\min_{\beta\in S,u\in S_{u}}\,\max_{v\in S_{v}}

\displaystyle\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{1}_{mk}^{\intercal}\,\rho(u)-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{y}^{\intercal}u+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2m}$}}\|P_{\Sigma}\beta\|^{2}_{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}v^{\intercal}(u-\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta)

\displaystyle\;-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}v^{\intercal}\mathbf{h}_{1}\|\beta\|_{\Sigma_{1}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\|v\|\mathbf{g}_{1}^{\intercal}\Sigma_{1}^{1/2}\beta

\displaystyle\;-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk^{3/2}}$}}v^{\intercal}J_{mk}\mathbf{h}_{2}\|\beta\|_{\Sigma_{2}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\|v\|_{J_{mk}}\mathbf{g}_{2}^{\intercal}\Sigma_{2}^{1/2}\beta\;.

(109)

For simplicity, we have abbreviated
\mathbf{y}\equiv\mathbf{y}(\mathbf{G}\Sigma_{o}^{1/2}\beta^{*})
.

Salehi et al. (2019)
showed that, under their CGMT result (analogous to our
5
(i) and
5
(ii)), the minimum and maximum can be exchanged in the auxiliary optimization in an asymptotic sense since they can be exchanged in the primary optimization. Throughout the analysis of AO, we will highlight explicitly where such flipping is done, and in the case where the min-max theorem is not applicable, we defer a rigorous justification to the end of the proof.

For simplicity of notation, given a matrix
A\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{\prime}\times d}
and a subset
S\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we also write
A(S)=\{Av\,|\,v\in S\}
for short.

Maximizing over
v\in S_{v}\subset\mathbb{R}^{mk}
.
Consider the projection matrix
P_{mk}\coloneqq\frac{1}{k}J_{mk}
and write
P^{\perp}_{mk}=I_{mk}-P_{mk}
. Notice also that
\|{\,\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}}\,}\|_{J_{mk}}=\sqrt{k}\,\|P_{mk}({\,\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}}\,})\|
. Then the maximization over
v
can be re-expressed as

\displaystyle\max_{P^{\perp}_{mk}v\in P^{\perp}_{mk}(S_{v})}\max_{P_{mk}v\in P_{mk}(S_{v})}\,\,

\displaystyle\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}v^{\intercal}P_{mk}(u-\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta-\mathbf{h}_{1}\|\beta\|_{\Sigma_{1}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{k}}$}}J_{mk}\mathbf{h}_{2}\|\beta\|_{\Sigma_{2}})

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}v^{\intercal}P^{\perp}_{mk}(u-\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta-\mathbf{h}_{1}\|\beta\|_{\Sigma_{1}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{k}}$}}J_{mk}\mathbf{h}_{2}\|\beta\|_{\Sigma_{2}})

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\|P_{mk}v\|\mathbf{g}_{1}\Sigma_{1}^{1/2}\beta+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\|P^{\perp}_{mk}v\|\mathbf{g}_{1}^{\intercal}\Sigma_{1}^{1/2}\beta+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{m \sqrt{k}}$}}\|P_{mk}v\|\mathbf{g}_{2}^{\intercal}\Sigma_{2}^{1/2}\beta\;.

Maximizing the above over
P_{mk}v
and
P^{\perp}_{mk}v
separately, choosing each vector to be what it multiplies and writing
r_{1}=\|P^{\perp}_{mk}v\|/\sqrt{mk}
and
r_{2}=\|P_{mk}v\|/\sqrt{mk}
(analogous to (44) – (45) in
Salehi et al. (2019)
), the above can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\max_{(r_{1},r_{2})\in S_{r_{1}}\times S_{r_{2}}}\,

\displaystyle\;r_{1}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{mk}}$}}\mathbf{g}_{1}^{\intercal}\Sigma_{1}^{1/2}\beta+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{mk}}$}}\big\|P^{\perp}_{mk}(u-\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta-\mathbf{h}_{1}\|\beta\|_{\Sigma_{1}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{k}}$}}J_{mk}\mathbf{h}_{2}\|\beta\|_{\Sigma_{2}})\big\|\Big)

\displaystyle\;+r_{2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{mk}}$}}\mathbf{g}_{1}^{\intercal}\Sigma_{1}^{1/2}\beta+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{m}}$}}\mathbf{g}_{2}^{\intercal}\Sigma_{2}^{1/2}\beta

\displaystyle\hskip 30.00005pt+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{mk}}$}}\big\|P_{mk}(u-\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta-\mathbf{h}_{1}\|\beta\|_{\Sigma_{1}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{k}}$}}J_{mk}\mathbf{h}_{2}\|\beta\|_{\Sigma_{2}})\big\|\Big)\;,

where we have denoted

\displaystyle S_{r_{1}}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\Big\{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{mk}}$}}\|P^{\perp}_{mk}v\|\,\Big|\,v\in S_{v}\Big\}

and

\displaystyle S_{r_{2}}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\Big\{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{mk}}$}}\|P_{mk}v\|\,\Big|\,v\in S_{v}\Big\}\;.

Substituting this into (
109
) yields

\displaystyle\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in S\\
u\in S_{u}\end{subarray}}\,\max_{(r_{1},r_{2})\in S_{r_{1}}\times S_{r_{2}}}

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{1}_{mk}^{\intercal}\,\rho(u)-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{y}^{\intercal}u+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2m}$}}\|P_{\Sigma}\beta\|^{2}_{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_1+r_2}{\sqrt{mk}}$}}\mathbf{g}_{1}^{\intercal}\Sigma_{1}^{1/2}\beta+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2}{\sqrt{n}}$}}\mathbf{g}_{2}^{\intercal}\Sigma_{2}^{1/2}\beta

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_1}{\sqrt{mk}}$}}\big\|P^{\perp}_{mk}(u-\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta-\mathbf{h}_{1}\|\beta\|_{\Sigma_{1}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{k}}$}}J_{mk}\mathbf{h}_{2}\|\beta\|_{\Sigma_{2}})\big\|

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2}{\sqrt{mk}}$}}\big\|P_{mk}(u-\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta-\mathbf{h}_{1}\|\beta\|_{\Sigma_{1}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{k}}$}}J_{mk}\mathbf{h}_{2}\|\beta\|_{\Sigma_{2}})\big\|\;.

Minimizing over
\beta\in S
.
As with (47) of
Salehi et al. (2019)
, we introduce new variables
\mu,w\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
to replace
\beta
in the regularization term via the Lagrange multiplier method applied to the constraint
P_{\Sigma}\mu=P_{\Sigma}\beta
:

\displaystyle\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in S\\
u\in S_{u}\\
\mu\in S\end{subarray}}\;\;\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in\mathbb{R}^{p}\\
(r_{1},r_{2})\in S_{r_{1}}\times S_{r_{2}}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{L}_{1}(\beta,u,\mu,w,r_{1},r_{2})\;,

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{1}(\beta,u,\mu,w,r_{1},r_{2})\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{1}_{mk}^{\intercal}\,\rho(u)-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{y}^{\intercal}u+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2m}$}}\|P_{\Sigma}\mu\|^{2}_{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{p}$}}w^{\intercal}P_{\Sigma}(\mu-\beta)

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_1+r_2}{\sqrt{mk}}$}}\mathbf{g}_{1}^{\intercal}\Sigma_{1}^{1/2}\beta+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2}{\sqrt{m}}$}}\mathbf{g}_{2}^{\intercal}\Sigma_{2}^{1/2}\beta

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_1}{\sqrt{mk}}$}}\big\|P^{\perp}_{mk}(u-\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta-\mathbf{h}_{1}\|\beta\|_{\Sigma_{1}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{k}}$}}J_{mk}\mathbf{h}_{2}\|\beta\|_{\Sigma_{2}})\big\|

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2}{\sqrt{mk}}$}}\big\|P_{mk}(u-\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta-\mathbf{h}_{1}\|\beta\|_{\Sigma_{1}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{k}}$}}J_{mk}\mathbf{h}_{2}\|\beta\|_{\Sigma_{2}})\big\|\;.

(110)

To minimize over
\beta\in S
, we first swap the order of
\min_{\beta\in S}
and
\max_{w\in\mathbb{R}^{p},\;(r_{1},r_{2})\in S_{r_{1}}\times S_{r_{2}}}
. Notice that the
\beta
-dependence in the loss comes from
P_{\Sigma}\beta
,
\Sigma^{1/2}_{1}\beta
,
\Sigma^{1/2}_{2}\beta
and
\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta
. Writing
\tilde{\beta}=\sqrt{p}\,P_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta
,
\tilde{\beta}^{\perp}=\sqrt{p}\,P_{*}^{\perp}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta
,
v(\beta^{*})\coloneqq\sqrt{p}\,\Sigma_{*}\Sigma_{o}^{1/2}\beta^{*}
and

\kappa_{*}\coloneqq\|\Sigma_{*}\Sigma_{o}^{1/2}\beta^{*}\|
, we can express

\displaystyle\Sigma^{1/2}_{1}\beta\;=

\displaystyle\;\big(\Sigma^{1/2}P_{*}^{\perp}(I_{p}-\Sigma_{*})P_{*}^{\perp}\Sigma^{1/2}\big)^{1/2}\beta\;\overset{(a)}{=}\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{p}}$}}(I_{p}-\Sigma_{*})\tilde{\beta}^{\perp}\;,

\displaystyle\Sigma^{1/2}_{2}\beta\;=

\displaystyle\;\big(\Sigma^{1/2}P_{*}^{\perp}\Sigma_{*}P_{*}^{\perp}\Sigma^{1/2}\big)^{1/2}\beta\;\overset{(b)}{=}\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{p}}$}}\Sigma_{*}\tilde{\beta}^{\perp}\;,

\displaystyle\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta\;=

\displaystyle\;\underbrace{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{p}}$}}\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}v(\beta^{*})}_{\eqqcolon\kappa_{*}\mathbf{q}}\,\times\,\underbrace{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{v(\beta^*)^\intercal\Sigma^{1/2} \beta}{\sqrt{p} \, \kappa_*^2}$}}}_{\eqqcolon\alpha(\tilde{\beta})}\;,

\displaystyle P_{\Sigma}\beta\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{p}}$}}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}\tilde{\beta}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{p}}$}}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}\tilde{\beta}^{\perp}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\alpha(\tilde\beta)}{\sqrt{p}}$}}\,(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}v(\beta^{*})+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{p}}$}}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}\tilde{\beta}^{\perp}\;.

In
(a)
and
(b)
above, we have used
Assumption

11
(ii) to note that
I_{p}-\Sigma_{*}
and
\Sigma_{*}
are both idempotent. This allows us to express all
\beta
-dependent terms in terms of
\alpha(\tilde{\beta})
and
\tilde{\beta}^{\perp}
, where
\tilde{\beta}
and
\tilde{\beta}^{\perp}
are orthogonal and can be optimized separately. Therefore, the optimization can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in S_{u}\\
\mu\in S\\
\alpha\in S^{\alpha}\end{subarray}}\,\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in\mathbb{R}^{p}\\
(r_{1},r_{2})\in S_{r_{1}}\times S_{r_{2}}\end{subarray}}\,\min_{\tilde{\beta}^{\perp}\in\tilde{S}^{\perp}}

\displaystyle\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{1}_{mk}^{\intercal}\,\rho(u)-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{y}^{\intercal}u+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2m}$}}\|P_{\Sigma}\mu\|^{2}_{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{p}$}}w^{\intercal}P_{\Sigma}\mu

\displaystyle\,\hskip-30.00005pt-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\alpha}{p \sqrt{p}}$}}\,w^{\intercal}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}v(\beta^{*})-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{p \sqrt{p}}$}}w^{\intercal}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}\tilde{\beta}^{\perp}

\displaystyle\,\hskip-30.00005pt+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_1 + r_2}{\sqrt{mkp}}$}}\mathbf{g}_{1}^{\intercal}(I_{p}-\Sigma_{*})\tilde{\beta}^{\perp}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2}{\sqrt{mp}}$}}\mathbf{g}_{2}^{\intercal}\Sigma_{*}\tilde{\beta}^{\perp}

\displaystyle\;\hskip-30.00005pt+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_1}{\sqrt{mk}}$}}\Big\|P^{\perp}_{mk}\Big(u-\kappa_{*}\alpha\mathbf{q}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{p}}$}}\mathbf{h}_{1}\|(I_{p}-\Sigma_{*})\tilde{\beta}^{\perp}\|-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{pk}}$}}J_{mk}\mathbf{h}_{2}\|\Sigma_{*}\tilde{\beta}^{\perp}\|\Big)\Big\|

\displaystyle\;\hskip-30.00005pt+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2}{\sqrt{mk}}$}}\Big\|P_{mk}\Big(u-\kappa_{*}\alpha\mathbf{q}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{p}}$}}\mathbf{h}_{1}\|(I_{p}-\Sigma_{*})\tilde{\beta}^{\perp}\|-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{pk}}$}}J_{mk}\mathbf{h}_{2}\|\Sigma_{*}\tilde{\beta}^{\perp}\|\Big)\Big\|\;,

(111)

where we have defined the sets

\displaystyle S^{\alpha}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\big\{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{v(\beta^*)^\intercal\Sigma^{1/2} \beta}{\sqrt{p} \, \kappa^2_*}$}}\,\big|\,\beta\in S\big\}

and

\displaystyle\tilde{S}^{\perp}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\{\sqrt{p}\,P^{\perp}_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta\,|\,\beta\in S\}\;.

Note that we have moved the minimization over
\alpha
to the outmost part of the loss. The steps so far are analogous to (46) – (47) of
Salehi et al. (2019)
. Before proceeding, we notice that since
I_{p}-\Sigma_{*}
and
\Sigma_{*}
are symmetric and idempotent by
Assumption

11
(ii), they are in fact projection matrices onto two orthogonal subspaces. Therefore to optimize the above over
\tilde{\beta}^{\perp}
, it suffices to do so over
(I_{p}-\Sigma_{*})\tilde{\beta}^{\perp}
and
\Sigma_{*}\tilde{\beta}^{\perp}
individually. Moreover, when optimizing over each of the projected
\tilde{\beta}^{\perp}
’s, the optimization takes exactly the same form as (47) of
Salehi et al. (2019)
. Similar to them, we introduce

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{p}}$}}\|(I_{p}-\Sigma_{*})\tilde{\beta}^{\perp}\|\;\in\;S_{\sigma_{1}}

and

\displaystyle\sigma_{2}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{p}}$}}\|\Sigma_{*}\tilde{\beta}^{\perp}\|\;\in\;S_{\sigma_{2}}\;,

where
S_{\sigma_{1}}\coloneqq\{\|(I_{p}-\Sigma_{*})P_{*}^{\perp}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta\|\,|\,\beta\in S\}
and
S_{\sigma_{2}}\coloneqq\{\|\Sigma_{*}P_{*}^{\perp}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta\|\,|\,\beta\in S\}
are both subsets of non-negative real numbers, as well as the auxiliary variables
\nu_{1},\nu_{2},\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\geq 0
. We also take note of the fact that

\displaystyle(I_{p}-\Sigma_{*})\tilde{\beta}^{\perp}\;\in

\displaystyle\;S_{\Sigma}^{\perp}\;\coloneqq\;\{\sqrt{p}\,(I_{p}-\Sigma_{*})P_{*}^{\perp}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta\,|\,\beta\in S\}\;,

\displaystyle\Sigma_{*}\tilde{\beta}^{\perp}\;\in

\displaystyle\;S_{\Sigma}\;\coloneqq\;\{\sqrt{p}\,\Sigma_{*}P_{*}^{\perp}P_{\Sigma}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta\,|\,\beta\in S\}\;,

and denote the projection onto
\textrm{span}(S_{\Sigma}^{\perp})
as
P_{S_{\Sigma}^{\perp}}
and the projection onto
\textrm{span}(S_{\Sigma})
as
P_{S_{\Sigma}}
. Then by the same algebra from (47) – (49) of
Salehi et al. (2019)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in S_{u}\\
\mu\in S\\
\alpha\in S^{\alpha}\\
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})\in S_{\sigma_{1}}\times S_{\sigma_{2}}\\
\nu_{1},\nu_{2}\geq 0\end{subarray}}\,\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in\mathbb{R}^{p}\\
(r_{1},r_{2})\in S_{r_{1}}\times S_{r_{2}}\\
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\geq 0\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{1}_{mk}^{\intercal}\,\rho(u)-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{y}^{\intercal}u+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2m}$}}\|P_{\Sigma}\mu\|^{2}_{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{p}$}}w^{\intercal}P_{\Sigma}\mu

\displaystyle\;-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\alpha}{p \sqrt{p}}$}}\,w^{\intercal}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}v(\beta^{*})

\displaystyle\;-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_1}{2 \tau_1}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_1 \tau_1}{2}$}}\,\Big\|P_{S_{\Sigma}^{\perp}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_1 + r_2}{\sqrt{mk}}$}}\mathbf{g}_{1}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{p}$}}(I_{p}-\Sigma_{*})(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}w\Big)\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_2}{2 \tau_2}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_2 \tau_2}{2}$}}\,\Big\|P_{S_{\Sigma}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2}{\sqrt{n}}$}}\mathbf{g}_{2}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{p}$}}\Sigma_{*}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}w\Big)\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_1}{2 \nu_1}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_1 \nu_1}{2 mk}$}}\Big\|P^{\perp}_{mk}\Big(u-\kappa_{*}\alpha\mathbf{q}-\sigma_{1}\mathbf{h}_{1}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_2}{\sqrt{k}}$}}J_{mk}\mathbf{h}_{2}\Big)\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2}{2 \nu_2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2 \nu_2}{ 2 mk}$}}\Big\|P_{mk}\Big(u-\kappa_{*}\alpha\mathbf{q}-\sigma_{1}\mathbf{h}_{1}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_2}{\sqrt{k}}$}}J_{mk}\mathbf{h}_{2}\Big)\Big\|^{2}\;.

(112)

Note that we have moved the maximization over
w,r_{1},r_{2}
to be inside the minimization over
\nu_{1}
,
\nu_{2}
,
\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{2}
. Note also that
P^{\perp}_{mk}\sigma_{2}J_{mk}\mathbf{h}_{2}
evaluates to zero, but we keep this term for the ease of computation later. We also remark that
\nu_{1}
can be restricted to be in a compact set
\big\{\big\|P^{\perp}_{mk}\big(u-\kappa_{*}\alpha\mathbf{q}-\sigma_{1}\mathbf{h}_{1}-\frac{\sigma_{2}}{\sqrt{k}}J_{mk}\mathbf{h}_{2}\big)\big\|\,\big|\,u\in S_{u}\big\}
for the purpose of flipping minimization and maximization, and so are
\nu_{2},\tau_{1},\tau_{2}
, but we do not do so for notational simplicity.

Maximization over
w\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
.
We first derive some useful relationships between the different projection matrices introduced so far: By the definition of
\Sigma_{*}
, we have

\displaystyle\Sigma_{*}P_{\Sigma}\;=\;(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}\text{\rm Cov}[\phi_{11}(X_{1}),\phi_{11}(X_{2})](\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}P_{\Sigma}\;=\;\Sigma_{*}\;.

(113)

Also by the definition of
P_{*}
and the idempotency of
\Sigma_{*}
,

\displaystyle P_{*}\Sigma_{*}\;=\;\Sigma_{*}P_{*}\;=\;\begin{cases}\Sigma_{*}\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(\Sigma_* \Sigma_o^{1/2} \beta^* ) (\Sigma_* \Sigma_o^{1/2} \beta^* )^\intercal}{\| \Sigma_* \Sigma_o^{1/2} \beta^* \|^2}$}}\;=\;P_{*}&\text{ if }\Sigma_{*}\Sigma_{o}^{1/2}\beta^{*}\neq 0\\
\Sigma_{*}\times 0\;=\;P_{*}&\text{ otherwise }\;.\end{cases}

(114)

This implies that

\displaystyle S_{\Sigma}^{\perp}\;=

\displaystyle\;\{\sqrt{p}\,(I_{p}-\Sigma_{*})(I_{p}-P_{*})\Sigma^{1/2}\beta\,|\,\beta\in S\}\;=\;\{\sqrt{p}\,(I_{p}-\Sigma_{*})\Sigma^{1/2}\beta\,|\,\beta\in S\}\;,

\displaystyle S_{\Sigma}\;=

\displaystyle\;\{\Sigma_{*}(I_{p}-P_{*})\Sigma^{1/2}\beta\,|\,\beta\in S\}\;=\;\{(\Sigma_{*}-P_{*})\Sigma^{1/2}\beta\,|\,\beta\in S\}\;,

and combining these with the assumption that
\textrm{span}(S)=\mathbb{R}^{p}
, we can express

\displaystyle P_{S_{\Sigma}^{\perp}}\;=

\displaystyle\;I_{p}-\Sigma_{*}

and

\displaystyle P_{S_{\Sigma}}\;=

\displaystyle\;\Sigma_{*}-P_{*}\;.

(115)

This in turn implies that

\displaystyle P_{S_{\Sigma}^{\perp}}(I_{p}-\Sigma_{*})=P_{S_{\Sigma}^{\perp}}\;,\qquad P_{S_{\Sigma}}\Sigma_{*}=P_{S_{\Sigma}^{\perp}}\;,\qquad P_{S_{\Sigma}^{\perp}}P_{S_{\Sigma}}=P_{*}P_{S_{\Sigma}^{\perp}}=P_{*}P_{S_{\Sigma}}=0\;,

(116)

and that

\displaystyle P_{\Sigma}w\;=

\displaystyle\;\Sigma^{1/2}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}w

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\Sigma^{1/2}P_{*}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}w+\Sigma^{1/2}P_{S_{\Sigma}^{\perp}}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}w+\Sigma^{1/2}P_{S_{\Sigma}}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}w

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\Sigma^{1/2}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{v(\beta^*) v(\beta^*)^\intercal}{p \kappa_*^2}$}}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}w+\Sigma^{1/2}P_{S_{\Sigma}^{\perp}}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}w+\Sigma^{1/2}P_{S_{\Sigma}}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}w\;.

Substituting these into (
112
), we obtain

\displaystyle\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in S_{u}\\
\mu\in S\\
\alpha\in S^{\alpha}\\
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})\in S_{\sigma_{1}}\times S_{\sigma_{2}}\\
\nu_{1},\nu_{2}\geq 0\end{subarray}}\,\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in\mathbb{R}^{p}\\
(r_{1},r_{2})\in S_{r_{1}}\times S_{r_{2}}\\
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\geq 0\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{1}_{mk}^{\intercal}\,\rho(u)-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{y}^{\intercal}u+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2n}$}}\|P_{\Sigma}\mu\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle\;+\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{p^2 \kappa_*^2}$}}\mu^{\intercal}\Sigma^{1/2}v(\beta^{*})-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\alpha}{p \sqrt{p}}$}}\,\Big)\,v(\beta^{*})^{\intercal}P_{*}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}w

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{p}$}}(\Sigma^{1/2}\mu)^{\intercal}P_{S_{\Sigma}^{\perp}}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}w+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{p}$}}(\Sigma^{1/2}\mu)^{\intercal}P_{S_{\Sigma}}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}w

\displaystyle\;-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_1}{2 \tau_1}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_1 \tau_1}{2}$}}\,\Big\|P_{S_{\Sigma}^{\perp}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_1 + r_2}{\sqrt{mk}}$}}\mathbf{g}_{1}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{p}$}}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}w\Big)\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_2}{2 \tau_2}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_2 \tau_2}{2}$}}\,\Big\|P_{S_{\Sigma}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2}{\sqrt{n}}$}}\mathbf{g}_{2}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{p}$}}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}w\Big)\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_1}{2 \nu_1}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_1 \nu_1}{2 mk}$}}\big\|P^{\perp}_{mk}(u-\kappa_{*}\alpha\mathbf{q}-\sigma_{1}\mathbf{h}_{1}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_2}{\sqrt{k}}$}}J_{mk}\mathbf{h}_{2})\big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2}{2 \nu_2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2 \nu_2}{ 2 mk}$}}\big\|P_{mk}(u-\kappa_{*}\alpha\mathbf{q}-\sigma_{1}\mathbf{h}_{1}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_2}{\sqrt{k}}$}}J_{mk}\mathbf{h}_{2})\big\|^{2}\;.

(117)

To optimize the above over
w
, it again suffices to optimize over three mutually orthogonal vectors
P_{*}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}w
,
P_{S_{\Sigma}^{\perp}}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}w
and
P_{S_{\Sigma}}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}w
. The optimization over
P_{*}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}w
is exactly analogous to the optimization over
\mathbf{P}\mathbf{w}
in (49) of
Salehi et al. (2019)
, whereas the optimization over the other two vectors are exactly analogous to that over
\mathbf{P}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}
in (49) of
Salehi et al. (2019)
. Therefore by the exact same completion-of-squares argument as in (49) – (51) in
Salehi et al. (2019)
but without taking the asymptotic approximation, the optimization becomes

\displaystyle\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in S_{u}\\
\mu\in S\\
\alpha\in S^{\alpha}\\
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})\in S_{\sigma_{1}}\times S_{\sigma_{2}}\\
\nu_{1},\nu_{2}\geq 0\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\mu^{\intercal}\Sigma^{1/2}v(\beta^{*})=\alpha\kappa_{*}^{2}\end{subarray}}\,\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}(r_{1},r_{2})\in S_{r_{1}}\times S_{r_{2}}\\
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\geq 0\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{1}_{mk}^{\intercal}\,\rho(u)-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{y}^{\intercal}u+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2m}$}}\|P_{\Sigma}\mu\|^{2}_{2}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_1}{2 \tau_1}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_2}{2 \tau_2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_1}{2 \nu_1}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2}{2 \nu_2}$}}

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2\sigma_1 \tau_1}$}}\|P_{S_{\Sigma}^{\perp}}\Sigma^{1/2}\mu\|^{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_1 + r_2}{\sqrt{mk}}$}}\mathbf{g}_{1}^{\intercal}P_{S_{\Sigma}^{\perp}}\Sigma^{1/2}\mu

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2\sigma_2 \tau_2}$}}\|P_{S_{\Sigma}}\Sigma^{1/2}\mu\|^{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2}{\sqrt{m}}$}}\mathbf{g}_{2}^{\intercal}P_{S_{\Sigma}}\Sigma^{1/2}\mu

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_1 \nu_1}{2 mk}$}}\big\|P^{\perp}_{mk}(u-\kappa_{*}\alpha\mathbf{q}-\sigma_{1}\mathbf{h}_{1}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_2}{\sqrt{k}}$}}J_{mk}\mathbf{h}_{2})\big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2 \nu_2}{ 2 mk}$}}\big\|P_{mk}(u-\kappa_{*}\alpha\mathbf{q}-\sigma_{1}\mathbf{h}_{1}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_2}{\sqrt{k}}$}}J_{mk}\mathbf{h}_{2})\big\|^{2}\;,

(118)

Rewriting the minimization over
\mu\in S
.

We now flip the order of optimization such that we can perform the minimization over
\mu
first. This involves computing

\displaystyle\;\min_{\mu\in S}\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2n}$}}\|P_{\Sigma}\mu\|^{2}_{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2\sigma_1 \tau_1}$}}\|P_{S_{\Sigma}^{\perp}}\Sigma^{1/2}\mu\|^{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_1 + r_2}{\sqrt{mk}}$}}\mathbf{g}_{1}^{\intercal}P_{S_{\Sigma}^{\perp}}\Sigma^{1/2}\mu

\displaystyle\;\qquad+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2\sigma_2 \tau_2}$}}\|P_{S_{\Sigma}}\Sigma^{1/2}\mu\|^{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2}{\sqrt{n}}$}}\mathbf{g}_{2}^{\intercal}P_{S_{\Sigma}}\Sigma^{1/2}\mu\hskip 30.00005pt\text{ s.t. }\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{p}}$}}\mu^{\intercal}\Sigma^{1/2}v(\beta^{*})=\alpha\kappa_{*}^{2}\;.

(119)

Recall from (
115
) that
P_{S_{\Sigma}^{\perp}}=I_{p}-\Sigma_{*}
and
P_{S_{\Sigma}}=\Sigma_{*}-P_{*}
. Denote

\displaystyle\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}^{c}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2 \sigma_1 \tau_1}$}}(P_{\Sigma}-\Sigma_{*})+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2 \sigma_2 \tau_2}$}}(\Sigma_{*}-P_{*})\;,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{g}}^{c}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_1 + r_2}{\sqrt{mk}}$}}\,(P_{\Sigma}-\Sigma_{*})\mathbf{g}_{1}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2}{\sqrt{m}}$}}\,(\Sigma_{*}-P_{*})\mathbf{g}_{2}\;.

Then the problem comes

\displaystyle\;\min_{\mu\in S}\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2n}$}}\|P_{\Sigma}\mu\|^{2}_{2}+(\Sigma^{1/2}\mu)^{\intercal}\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}^{c}(\Sigma^{1/2}\mu)-(\tilde{\mathbf{g}}^{c})^{\intercal}\Sigma^{1/2}\mu\hskip 30.00005pt\text{ s.t. }\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{p}}$}}\mu^{\intercal}\Sigma^{1/2}v(\beta^{*})=\alpha\kappa_{*}^{2}\;.

By (
114
) and (
113
),
P_{\Sigma}P_{*}=P_{\Sigma}\Sigma_{*}P_{*}=\Sigma_{*}P_{*}=P_{*}
and by (
115
),
P_{S_{\Sigma}^{\perp}}=I_{p}-\Sigma_{*}
and
P_{S_{\Sigma}}=\Sigma_{*}-P_{*}
. Then by a similar argument as (
117
), we may express

\displaystyle P_{\Sigma}\;=\;P_{\Sigma}(P_{*}+P_{S_{\Sigma}^{\perp}}+P_{S_{\Sigma}})\;=\;P_{*}+(P_{\Sigma}-\Sigma_{*})+(\Sigma_{*}-P_{*})\;,

(120)

where
P_{*}
,
P_{\Sigma}-\Sigma_{*}
and
\Sigma_{*}-P_{*}
are projections onto mutually orthogonal subspaces. Meanwhile, recalling the definition of
\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{g}}
, we can express

\displaystyle\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2 \sigma_1 \tau_1}$}}(P_{\Sigma}-\Sigma_{*})+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2 \sigma_2 \tau_2}$}}\Sigma_{*}\;=\;\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}^{c}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2 \sigma_2 \tau_2}$}}P_{*}\;,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{g}}\;=

\displaystyle\;-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_1 + r_2}{\sqrt{mk}}$}}\,(P_{\Sigma}-\Sigma_{*})\mathbf{g}_{1}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2}{\sqrt{m}}$}}\,\Sigma_{*}\mathbf{g}_{2}\;=\;\tilde{\mathbf{g}}^{c}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2}{\sqrt{m}}$}}P_{*}\mathbf{g}_{2}\;.

Recalling also that
P_{*}=v(\beta^{*})v(\beta^{*})^{\intercal}/(p\kappa_{*}^{2})
, we can write

\displaystyle\;(\Sigma^{1/2}\mu)^{\intercal}\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}^{c}(\Sigma^{1/2}\mu)-(\tilde{\mathbf{g}}^{c})^{\intercal}\Sigma^{1/2}\mu

\displaystyle\;=\;(\Sigma^{1/2}\mu)^{\intercal}\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}(\Sigma^{1/2}\mu)-\tilde{\mathbf{g}}^{\intercal}\Sigma^{1/2}\mu-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2 \sigma_2 \tau_2}$}}(\Sigma^{1/2}\mu)^{\intercal}P_{*}(\Sigma^{1/2}\mu)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2}{\sqrt{n}}$}}\big(P_{*}\mathbf{g}_{2}\big)^{\intercal}\Sigma^{1/2}\mu

\displaystyle\;=\;(\Sigma^{1/2}\mu)^{\intercal}\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}(\Sigma^{1/2}\mu)-\tilde{\mathbf{g}}^{\intercal}\Sigma^{1/2}\mu-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\alpha^2 \kappa_*^2}{2 \sigma_2 \tau_2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2}{\sqrt{m}}$}}\mathbf{g}_{2}^{\intercal}P_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}\mu\;.

Now using a Lagrange multiplier
\theta
to remove the constraint, the optimization becomes

\displaystyle\min_{\mu\in S}\,\max_{\theta\in\mathbb{R}}\,

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2n}$}}\|P_{\Sigma}\mu\|^{2}_{2}+(\Sigma^{1/2}\mu)^{\intercal}\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}(\Sigma^{1/2}\mu)-\Big(\tilde{\mathbf{g}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta}{\sqrt{p}}$}}v(\beta^{*})\Big)^{\intercal}\Sigma^{1/2}\mu

\displaystyle\;-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\alpha^2 \kappa_*^2}{2 \sigma_2 \tau_2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2}{\sqrt{m}}$}}\mathbf{g}_{2}^{\intercal}P_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}\mu+\alpha\theta\kappa_{*}^{2}\;.

Since the problem is convex-concave, we can apply the min-max theorem of
Rockafellar (1970)
to flip the order of minimum and maximum. Doing this together with a completion of squares, we obtain

\displaystyle\max_{\theta\in\mathbb{R}}\,M_{\mathbf{g},\sigma,\tau,\theta}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{4}$}}\big\|(\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}^{\dagger})^{1/2}\big(\tilde{\mathbf{g}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta}{\sqrt{p}}$}}v(\beta^{*})\big)\big\|^{2}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\alpha^2 \kappa_*^2}{2 \sigma_2 \tau_2}$}}+\alpha\theta\kappa_{*}^{2}\;,

(121)

where we have denoted the Moreau envelope like term

\displaystyle M_{\mathbf{g},\sigma,\tau,\theta}\;\coloneqq\;\min_{\mu\in S}\,

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2n}$}}\|P_{\Sigma}\mu\|^{2}_{2}+\big\|\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}^{1/2}(\Sigma^{1/2}\mu)-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}(\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}^{\dagger})^{1/2}\big(\tilde{\mathbf{g}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta}{\sqrt{p}}$}}v(\beta^{*})\big)\big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2}{\sqrt{m}}$}}\mathbf{g}_{2}^{\intercal}P_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}\mu\;.

Rewriting the minimization over
u\in S_{u}
.
Meanwhile, the minimization over
u\in S_{u}
involves

\displaystyle\min_{u\in S_{u}}\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{1}_{mk}^{\intercal}\,\rho(u)-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{y}^{\intercal}u+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_1 \nu_1}{2 mk}$}}\big\|P^{\perp}_{mk}(u-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma})\big\|^{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2 \nu_2}{ 2mk}$}}\big\|P_{mk}(u-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma})\big\|^{2}\;,

(122)

where we have used the shorthand
\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma}=\kappa_{*}\alpha\mathbf{q}-\sigma_{1}\mathbf{h}_{1}-\frac{\sigma_{2}}{\sqrt{k}}J_{mk}\mathbf{h}_{2}
. Recall that by definition,
\mathbf{y}=P_{mk}\mathbf{y}
since
\mathbf{y}
is a length-
mk
vector formed by
k
-fold repetitions of
m
entries. Then we can re-express the loss above as

\displaystyle\;\min_{u\in S_{u}}\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{1}_{mk}^{\intercal}\,\rho(u)-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{y}^{\intercal}P_{mk}u+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_1 \nu_1}{2 mk}$}}\big\|P^{\perp}_{mk}(u-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma})\big\|^{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2 \nu_2}{ 2 mk}$}}\big\|P_{mk}(u-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma})\big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\min_{u\in S_{u}}\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{1}_{mk}^{\intercal}\,\rho(P_{mk}u+P_{mk}^{\perp}u)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2 \nu_2}{2 mk}$}}\Big\|P_{mk}\big(u-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{r_2 \nu_2}$}}\mathbf{y}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma}\big)\Big\|^{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_1 \nu_1}{ 2 mk}$}}\big\|P^{\perp}_{mk}(u-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma})\big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2 r_2 \nu_2 mk }$}}\big\|P_{mk}\mathbf{y}\big\|^{2}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{y}^{\intercal}P_{mk}\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma}\;.

The loss can therefore be minimized separately in
P_{mk}u
and
P_{mk}^{\perp}u
. Recall that for a function
f:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
and
\mathcal{S}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{mk}
, we defined the Moreau envelope

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{S}(f;v,t)\;\coloneqq\;\min_{x\in\mathcal{S}}f(x)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2 t}$}}\|x-v\|^{2}_{2}\;,

Also recall the definition

\displaystyle M^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma},r,\nu}(\tilde{u})\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathcal{M}_{P_{mk}^{\perp}(S_{u})}\big(\mathbf{1}_{mk}^{\intercal}\,\rho(\tilde{u}+{\,\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}}\,})\,;\,P^{\perp}_{mk}\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma},\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{r_1 \nu_1}$}}\big)\;,

\displaystyle M_{\mathbf{y},\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma},r,\nu}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathcal{M}_{P_{mk}(S_{u})}\big(M^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma},r,\nu}\,;\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{r_2 \nu_2}$}}\mathbf{y}-P_{mk}\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma}\,,\,r_{2}\nu_{2}\big)\;.

Then (
122
) can be expressed as

\displaystyle\;\min_{\tilde{u}\in P_{mk}(S_{u})}\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}M^{\perp}_{\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma},r,\nu}(\tilde{u})+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2 \nu_2}{2 mk}$}}\Big\|P_{mk}\big(\tilde{u}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{r_1 \nu_1}$}}\mathbf{y}-\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma}\big)\Big\|^{2}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2 r_2 \nu_2 mk }$}}\big\|P_{mk}\mathbf{y}\big\|^{2}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{y}^{\intercal}P_{mk}\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma}

\displaystyle\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}M_{\mathbf{y},\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma},r,\nu}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2 r_2 \nu_2 mk }$}}\|\mathbf{y}\|^{2}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{y}^{\intercal}\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma}\;,

(123)

where we have used
P_{mk}\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{y}
again in the last line. Substituting both (
121
) and (
123
) into (
118
) yields

\displaystyle\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in S^{\alpha}\\
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})\in S_{\sigma_{1}}\times S_{\sigma_{2}}\\
\nu_{1},\nu_{2}\geq 0\end{subarray}}\,\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}(r_{1},r_{2})\in S_{r_{1}}\times S_{r_{2}}\\
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\geq 0\\
\theta\in\mathbb{R}\end{subarray}}\,

\displaystyle\,-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_1}{2 \tau_1}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_2}{2 \tau_2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_1}{2 \nu_1}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2}{2 \nu_2}$}}+\alpha\theta\kappa_{*}^{2}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\alpha^2 \kappa_*^2}{2 \sigma_2 \tau_2}$}}

\displaystyle\,+M_{\mathbf{g},\sigma,\tau,\theta}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{4}$}}\big\|(\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}^{\dagger})^{1/2}\big(\tilde{\mathbf{g}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta}{\sqrt{p}}$}}v(\beta^{*})\big)\big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\,+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}M_{\mathbf{y},\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma},r,\nu}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2 r_2 \nu_2 mk }$}}\|\mathbf{y}\|^{2}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{y}^{\intercal}\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma}\;,

which equals
R^{\rm SO}_{S,S_{u},S_{v}}
.

Justifying the flipping of the minima and maxima.
To conclude, we need to justify the flipping of min-max in the analysis of the auxiliary optimization above. The same argument has been performed for the logistic loss in the isotropic, unaugmented case in
Salehi et al. (2019)
and in more details for general losses in
Thrampoulidis et al. (2018)
. For completeness, we repeat the arguments of the proof of Lemma A.3 of
Thrampoulidis et al. (2018)
in our context to illustrate how the non-asymptotic inequalities arise in our result for one particular flipping, and refer readers to Appendix A of
Thrampoulidis et al. (2018)
for more details in the general setup.

We now consider the flipping of
\min_{\beta\in S}
and
\max_{(r_{1},r_{2})\in S_{r_{1}}\times S_{r_{2}}}
in (
110
). First define the loss function
\mathcal{L}_{1}(\beta,u,\mu,w,r_{1},r_{2})
as in (
110
) and denote the risk at (
110
) by

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in S\\
u\in\mathcal{S}_{u}\\
\mu\in\mathcal{S}\end{subarray}}\;\;\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in\mathbb{R}^{p}\\
(r_{1},r_{2})\in S_{r_{1}}\times S_{r_{2}}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{L}_{1}(\beta,u,\mu,w,r_{1},r_{2})\;.

For convenience, we also abbreviate the dependence on random variables in

\displaystyle R^{\rm PO}_{S,S_{u},S_{v}}\;=

\displaystyle\;R^{\rm PO}_{S,S_{u},S_{v}}(\mathbf{G}\Sigma^{1/2}_{o}\beta^{*},\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}\Sigma^{1/2})\;,

\displaystyle L^{\rm PO}_{\beta,u,v}\;=

\displaystyle\;L^{\rm PO}_{\beta,u,v}(\mathbf{G}\Sigma^{1/2}_{o}\beta^{*},\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}\Sigma^{1/2})\;,

\displaystyle L^{\rm AO}_{\beta,u,v}\;=

\displaystyle\;L^{\rm AO}_{\beta,u,v}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{G}^{\Phi}P_{*},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{h})\;.

By the computation of the auxiliary formulation up to (
110
), and by
48
, we can apply
5
(i) and (ii) to obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(R^{\rm PO}_{S,S_{u},S_{v}}\;\leq\;c\big)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;4\,\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{R}_{1}\;\leq\;c\big)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(R^{\rm PO}_{S,S_{u},S_{v}}\;\geq\;c\big)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;4\,\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{R}_{1}\;\geq\;c\big)

(124)

for all
c\in\mathbb{R}
. Now define

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{1}\;\coloneqq\;\max_{(r_{1},r_{2})\in S_{r_{1}}\times S_{r_{2}}}\;\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in S\\
u\in\mathcal{S}_{u}\\
\mu\in\mathcal{S}\end{subarray}}\;\;\max_{w\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}\mathcal{L}_{1}(\beta,u,\mu,w,r_{1},r_{2})\;.

By the min-max inequality (
Rockafellar (1970)
, Lemma 36.1), we have

\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{1}\leq\mathcal{R}_{1}
and therefore

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(R^{\rm PO}_{S,S_{u},S_{v}}\;\leq\;c\big)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;4\,\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{R}_{1}\;\leq\;c\big)\;\leq\;4\,\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{1}\;\leq\;c\big)\;.

(125)

To relate
\{R^{\rm PO}_{S,S_{u},S_{v}}\geq c\}
to
\{\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{1}\geq c\}
, we apply the min-max theorem (
Rockafellar (1970)
, Corollary 37.3.2) to obtain that

\displaystyle R^{\rm PO}_{S,S_{u},S_{v}}\;=\;\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in S\\
u\in S_{u}\end{subarray}}\,\max_{v\in S_{v}}\,L^{\rm PO}_{\beta,u,v}\;=\;\max_{v\in S_{v}}\,\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in S\\
u\in S_{u}\end{subarray}}\,L^{\rm PO}_{\beta,u,v}\;,

and applying
5
gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(R^{\rm PO}_{S,S_{u},S_{v}}\geq c)\;\leq\;4\,\mathbb{P}\bigg(\max_{v\in S_{v}}\,\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in S\\
u\in S_{u}\end{subarray}}L^{\rm AO}_{\beta,u,v}\,\geq\,c\bigg)\;.

(126)

Now recall that

\displaystyle S_{r_{1}}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\Big\{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{mk}}$}}\|P_{mk}v\|\,\Big|\,v\in S_{v}\Big\}

and

\displaystyle S_{r_{2}}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\Big\{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{mk}}$}}\|P^{\perp}_{mk}v\|\,\Big|\,v\in S_{v}\Big\}\;.

Defining
\tilde{S}_{v}(r_{1},r_{2})\coloneqq\{v\in S_{v}\,\big|\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{mk}}\|P_{mk}v\|=r_{1}\,,\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{mk}}\|P^{\perp}_{mk}v\|=r_{2}\}
, we can rewrite

\displaystyle\max_{v\in S_{v}}\,\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in S\\
u\in S_{u}\end{subarray}}L^{\rm AO}_{\beta,u,v}\;=

\displaystyle\;\max_{(r_{1},r_{2})\in S_{r_{1}}\times S_{r_{2}}}\,\max_{\tilde{v}_{1}\in\tilde{S}_{v}(r_{1},r_{2})}\,\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in S\\
u\in S_{u}\end{subarray}}\,\,L^{\rm AO}_{\beta,u,\tilde{v}}

\displaystyle\;\overset{(a)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\;\max_{(r_{1},r_{2})\in S_{r_{1}}\times S_{r_{2}}}\,\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in S\\
u\in S_{u}\end{subarray}}\,\max_{\tilde{v}\in\tilde{S}_{v}(r_{1},r_{2})}\,L^{\rm AO}_{\beta,u,\tilde{v}}\;\overset{(b)}{=}\;\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{1}\;,

where we have applied the min-max inequality (
Rockafellar (1970)
, Lemma 36.1) in
(a)
followed by the same computation up to (
110
) to maximize the loss over
\tilde{v}
. Combining this with (
126
) gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(R^{\rm PO}_{S,S_{u},S_{v}}\geq c)\;\leq\;4\,\mathbb{P}\bigg(\max_{v\in S_{v}}\,\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in S\\
u\in S_{u}\end{subarray}}L^{\rm AO}_{\beta,u,v}\,\geq\,c\bigg)\;\leq\;4\,\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{1}\,\geq\,c)\;.

Together with (
125
), this shows that
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{1}
is equivalent to
\mathcal{R}_{1}
in the sense that the CGMT inequalities of (
124
) hold also with
\mathcal{R}_{1}
replaced by
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{1}
, therefore justifying the flipping of the minimum and the maximum. The remaining flipping of minimum and maximum over compact sets hold for the same reason, and any flipping that involves the Lagrange multiplier
w\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
in (
110
) can be done in a similar manner by introducing the Lagrange multiplier directly to the (
PO
). This proves the first statement that for all
c\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(R^{\rm PO}_{S,S_{u},S_{v}}\leq c)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;4\,\mathbb{P}(R^{\rm SO}_{S,S_{u},S_{v}}\,\leq\,c)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(R^{\rm PO}_{S,S_{u},S_{v}}\geq c)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;4\,\mathbb{P}(R^{\rm SO}_{S,S_{u},S_{v}}\,\geq\,c)\;.

Partial statement when
S
is replaced by
S_{c,\epsilon}
.
When the optimization is over
S_{c,\epsilon}
, which is no longer compact, we cannot apply the min-max theorem for flipping min and max that involve
S_{c,\epsilon}
. However, notice that this change only affects optimizations over
\beta
,
\alpha
,
\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{2}
. For the optimization over
\beta
, we have shown in (
125
) that for the desired partial bound, the flipping of min and max does not require the min-max theorem. For the optimizations over
\alpha
,
\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{2}
, notice that the new domains of optimization for each of these variables are

\displaystyle\Big\{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{v(\beta^*)^\intercal\Sigma^{1/2} \beta}{\sqrt{p} \, \kappa^2_*}$}}\,\Big|\,\beta\in S\Big\}\;,\quad\big\{\|(I_{p}-\Sigma_{*})P_{*}^{\perp}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta\|\,\big|\,\beta\in S\big\}\;,\quad\big\{\|\Sigma_{*}P_{*}^{\perp}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta\|\,\big|\,\beta\in S\big\}\;,

which are the 1d images of continuous functions on
\mathbb{R}^{p}
. Since
S_{c,\epsilon}
is connected, the above sets are connected and therefore convex since they are one-dimensional. Therefore the replacement of
S
by
S_{c,\epsilon}
does not affect the application of min-max theorem that concerns
\alpha
,
\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{2}
. This proves the partial upper bound analogous to (
125
): For all
c\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(R^{\rm PO}_{S_{c,\epsilon},S_{u},S_{v}}\leq c)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;4\,\mathbb{P}(R^{\rm SO}_{S_{c,\epsilon},S_{u},S_{v}}\,\leq\,c)\;.

\jmlrQED

N.3
Proof of
50
: Equivalence between (
SO
) and (
DO
)

It is convenient to restate the optimization (
SO
):

\displaystyle\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in S^{\alpha}\\
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})\in S_{\sigma_{1}}\times S_{\sigma_{2}}\\
\nu_{1},\nu_{2}\geq 0\end{subarray}}\,\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}(r_{1},r_{2})\in S_{r_{1}}\times S_{r_{2}}\\
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\geq 0\\
\theta\in\mathbb{R}\end{subarray}}\,

\displaystyle\,-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_1}{2 \tau_1}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_2}{2 \tau_2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_1}{2 \nu_1}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2}{2 \nu_2}$}}+\alpha\theta\kappa_{*}^{2}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\alpha^2 \kappa_*^2}{2 \sigma_2 \tau_2}$}}

\displaystyle\,+M_{\mathbf{g},\sigma,\tau,\theta}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{4}$}}\big\|(\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}^{\dagger})^{1/2}\big(\tilde{\mathbf{g}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta}{\sqrt{p}}$}}v(\beta^{*})\big)\big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\,+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}M_{\mathbf{y},\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma},r,\nu}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2 r_2 \nu_2 mk }$}}\|\mathbf{y}\|^{2}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{mk}$}}\mathbf{y}^{\intercal}\tilde{\mathbf{h}}_{\alpha,\sigma}\;,

As with
Salehi et al. (2019)
, we exploit the fact that the optimization is over finitely many one-dimensional variables. It therefore suffices to analyze the asymptotics of the loss function directly, as one may first approximate the minimization and maximization over
(\tilde{\alpha},\tilde{\theta})
by a smooth function and then take the approximation error to zero as
m,p\rightarrow\infty
.

Compute terms involving
\mathbf{g}_{1}
and
\mathbf{g}_{2}
.
Recall that
\mathbf{g}_{1}
and
\mathbf{g}_{2}
are independent standard Gaussian
\mathbb{R}^{p}
vectors, and that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{g}}\;=

\displaystyle\;-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_1 + r_2}{\sqrt{mk}}$}}\,(P_{\Sigma}-\Sigma_{*})\mathbf{g}_{1}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2}{\sqrt{m}}$}}\,\Sigma_{*}\mathbf{g}_{2}\;,

\displaystyle\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2 \sigma_1 \tau_1}$}}(P_{\Sigma}-\Sigma_{*})+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2 \sigma_2 \tau_2}$}}\Sigma_{*}\;,

\displaystyle M_{\mathbf{g},\sigma,\tau,\theta}\;=

\displaystyle\;\min_{\mu\in S}\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2n}$}}\|P_{\Sigma}\mu\|^{2}_{2}+\big\|\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}^{1/2}(\Sigma^{1/2}\mu)-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}(\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}^{\dagger})^{1/2}\big(\tilde{\mathbf{g}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta}{\sqrt{p}}$}}v(\beta^{*})\big)\big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;\qquad+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2}{\sqrt{m}}$}}\mathbf{g}_{2}^{\intercal}P_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}\mu\;.

We focus on handling
M_{\mathbf{g},\sigma,\tau,\theta}
. First note that
\frac{r_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\mathbf{g}_{2}^{\intercal}P_{*}\Sigma^{1/2}\mu
depends on
\mu
through the scalar
v(\beta^{*})\Sigma^{1/2}\mu
, so by a similar reasoning as above, we can apply the law of large numbers directly to this term and obtain that it converges to zero in probability. Using
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
to denote terms that converge in probability to zero, we then have

\displaystyle M_{\mathbf{g},\sigma,\tau,\theta}\;=\;

\displaystyle\;o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)+\min_{\mu\in S}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2m}$}}\|P_{\Sigma}\mu\|^{2}_{2}+\big\|\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}^{1/2}(\Sigma^{1/2}\mu)-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}(\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}^{\dagger})^{1/2}\big(\tilde{\mathbf{g}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta}{\sqrt{p}}$}}v(\beta^{*})\big)\big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\tilde{M}_{\mathbf{g},\sigma,\tau,\theta}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{4}$}}\Big\|(\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}^{\dagger})^{1/2}\big(\tilde{\mathbf{g}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta}{\sqrt{p}}$}}v(\beta^{*})\big)\Big\|^{2}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)\;,

(127)

where

\displaystyle\tilde{M}_{\mathbf{g},\sigma,\tau,\theta}\;\coloneqq\;\min_{\mu\in S}(\Sigma^{1/2}\mu)^{\intercal}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2n}$}}\Sigma^{\dagger}+\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}\Big)(\Sigma^{1/2}\mu)-(\Sigma^{1/2}\mu)^{\intercal}\big(\tilde{\mathbf{g}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta}{\sqrt{p}}$}}v(\beta^{*})\big)\;.

The second term of (
127
) cancels with the other
(\mathbf{g}_{1},\mathbf{g}_{2})
-dependent term in the overall loss, so the only remaining
(\mathbf{g}_{1},\mathbf{g}_{2})
-dependent term is
\tilde{M}_{\mathbf{g},\sigma,\tau,\theta}
. By a completion of squares, we obtain

\displaystyle\tilde{M}_{\mathbf{g},\sigma,\tau,\theta}\;=\;\min_{\mu\in S}

\displaystyle\;\Big\|\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2m}$}}\Sigma^{\dagger}+\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}\Big)^{1/2}(\Sigma^{1/2}\mu)-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\Big(\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2m}$}}\Sigma^{\dagger}+\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}\Big)^{\dagger}\Big)^{1/2}\big(\tilde{\mathbf{g}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta}{\sqrt{p}}$}}v(\beta^{*})\big)\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{4}$}}\big(\tilde{\mathbf{g}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta}{\sqrt{p}}$}}v(\beta^{*})\big)^{\intercal}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2m}$}}\Sigma^{\dagger}+\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}\Big)^{\dagger}\big(\tilde{\mathbf{g}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta}{\sqrt{p}}$}}v(\beta^{*})\big)\;.

The second term does not involve
\mu
, so we first seek to take a limit of this term. Recall from (
120
) that
P_{*}
,
P_{\Sigma}-\Sigma_{*}
and
\Sigma_{*}-P_{*}
are projections onto mutually orthogonal subspaces and that
P_{\Sigma}\Sigma_{*}=\Sigma_{*}
,
P_{\Sigma}P_{*}=P_{*}
. We can then express

\displaystyle\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2m}$}}\Sigma^{\dagger}+\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}\Big)^{\dagger}\;=

\displaystyle\;\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2m}$}}\Sigma^{\dagger}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2 \sigma_1 \tau_1}$}}(P_{\Sigma}-\Sigma_{*})+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2 \sigma_2 \tau_2}$}}\Sigma_{*}\Big)^{\dagger}\;,

which implies that

\displaystyle\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2m}$}}\Sigma^{\dagger}+\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}\Big)^{\dagger}\big(\tilde{\mathbf{g}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta}{\sqrt{p}}$}}v(\beta^{*})\big)\;=

\displaystyle\;-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2(r_1+r_2)\sigma_1\tau_1}{\sqrt{mk}}$}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2\sigma_1 \tau_1 \lambda}{2m}$}}\Sigma^{\dagger}+I_{p}\Big)^{\dagger}(P_{\Sigma}-\Sigma_{*})\mathbf{g}_{1}

\displaystyle\;-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 r_2 \sigma_2\tau_2}{\sqrt{m}}$}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2\sigma_2 \tau_2 \lambda}{2m}$}}\Sigma^{\dagger}+I_{p}\Big)^{\dagger}\Sigma_{*}\mathbf{g}_{2}

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 \sigma_2 \tau_2 \theta}{\sqrt{p}}$}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 \sigma_2 \tau_2 \lambda}{2m}$}}\Sigma^{\dagger}+I_{p}\Big)^{\dagger}v(\beta^{*})

\displaystyle\;+O\big(\|m^{-1/2}P_{*}\mathbf{g}_{1}\|+\|m^{-1/2}P_{*}\mathbf{g}_{2}\|\big)\;.

(128)

Also notice that any term linear in
\mathbf{g}_{1}
or
\mathbf{g}_{2}
has expectation zero, which vanishes. Recall also that
v(\beta^{*})=\sqrt{p}\Sigma_{*}\Sigma_{o}^{1/2}\beta^{*}
and
\kappa_{*}=\|\Sigma_{*}\Sigma_{o}^{1/2}\beta^{*}\|
.
Computing the inverse along each orthogonal subspace explicitly and taking a limit with
m,p\rightarrow\infty
and
p/n=p/(mk)\rightarrow\kappa
, we obtain

\displaystyle-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{4}$}}\big(\tilde{\mathbf{g}}+

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta}{\sqrt{p}}$}}v(\beta^{*})\big)^{\intercal}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2m}$}}\Sigma^{\dagger}+\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}\Big)^{\dagger}\big(\tilde{\mathbf{g}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta}{\sqrt{p}}$}}v(\beta^{*})\big)

\displaystyle\;\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\rightarrow}

\displaystyle\;-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(r_1 + r_2)^2 \sigma_1 \tau_1}{2k}$}}\,\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{11}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2^2 \sigma_2 \tau_2}{2}$}}\,\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{12}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta^2 \bar\kappa_*^2 \sigma_2 \tau_2}{2}$}}\,\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{13}\;=\;-\bar{\chi}^{r,\theta,\sigma,\tau}_{1}\;.

where we have recalled that
P_{*}=v(\beta^{*})v(\beta^{*})^{\intercal}/(p\kappa_{*}^{2})
,
\bar{\kappa}_{*}=\lim\kappa_{*}
and

\displaystyle\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{11}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\lim\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\Tr\big(
\big( \frac{2 \sigma_1 \tau_1\lambda}{2m} \Sigma^\dagger+
I_p \big)^\dagger(P_\Sigma- \Sigma_*)
\big)
}{m}$}}\;,

\displaystyle\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{12}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\lim\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\Tr\big(
\big(
\frac{2 \sigma_2 \tau_2\lambda}{2m} \Sigma^\dagger+
I_p
\big)^\dagger\Sigma_*
\big)
}{m}$}}\;,

\displaystyle\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{13}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\lim\Tr\Big(
\Big(
\mfrac{2 \sigma_2 \tau_2\lambda}{2m} \Sigma^\dagger+
I_p
\Big)^\dagger P_*
\Big)\;.

To address the minimization over
\mu\in S
, notice that the only difference between the two choices of
S
are via the restriction on
\mu^{\intercal}\Sigma_{\rm new}\mu
. Recall that the two different choices of
S
differs only in
\beta^{\intercal}\Sigma_{\rm new}\beta
. Let
P_{\Sigma_{\rm new}}
be the projection onto the positive eigenspace of
P_{\Sigma_{\rm new}}
and
P_{\Sigma_{\rm new}}^{\perp}=I_{p}-P_{\Sigma_{\rm new}}
. Then we can rewrite the minimization as

\displaystyle\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\in P_{\Sigma_{\rm new}}(S)\\
\mu^{\prime}\in P_{\Sigma_{\rm new}}^{\perp}(S)\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\;\Big\|\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2m}$}}\Sigma^{\dagger}+\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}\Big)^{1/2}\Sigma^{1/2}\Big(\mu+\mu^{\prime}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2m}$}}\Sigma^{\dagger}+\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}\Big)^{\dagger}\big(\tilde{\mathbf{g}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta}{\sqrt{p}}$}}v(\beta^{*})\big)\Big)\Big\|^{2}\;.

With either choice of
S
,
\mu^{\prime}
can be chosen freely within
P^{\perp}_{\Sigma_{\rm new}}(\mathbb{R}^{p})
so long as
\|\mu^{\prime}\|_{2}=O(\sqrt{p})
. Minimizing over
\mu^{\prime}
first and noting that
P_{\Sigma_{\rm new}}=(\Sigma_{\rm new}^{\dagger})^{1/2}\Sigma_{\rm new}^{1/2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\min_{\mu\in P_{\Sigma_{\rm new}}(S)}

\displaystyle\;\Big\|\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2m}$}}\Sigma^{\dagger}+\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}\Big)^{1/2}\Sigma^{1/2}(\Sigma_{\rm new}^{\dagger})^{1/2}\Big(\Sigma_{\rm new}^{1/2}\mu-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\Sigma_{\rm new}^{1/2}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2m}$}}\Sigma^{\dagger}+\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}\Big)^{\dagger}\big(\tilde{\mathbf{g}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta}{\sqrt{p}}$}}v(\beta^{*})\big)\Big)\Big\|^{2}\;.

Setting
\Sigma_{\rm new}^{1/2}\mu
in the direction of minimization, we have that for some
c(\mu)\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\rm new}^{1/2}\mu\;=

\displaystyle\;c(\mu)\mathbf{g}^{\prime}\;,\qquad\mathbf{g}^{\prime}\;\coloneqq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\Sigma_{\rm new}^{1/2}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2m}$}}\Sigma^{\dagger}+\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}\Big)^{\dagger}\big(\tilde{\mathbf{g}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta}{\sqrt{p}}$}}v(\beta^{*})\big)\;,

which allows us to rewrite

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\Big\|
\Big( \mfrac{\lambda}{2m} \Sigma^\dagger+ \tilde\Sigma_{\sigma, \tau}
\Big)^{1/2}
\Sigma^{1/2} (\Sigma_{\rm new}^\dagger)^{1/2} \bg'
\Big\|^2}{\| \bg' \|^2}$}}\;\times\;\min_{\mu\in P_{\Sigma_{\rm new}}(S)}\;(c(\mu)\|\mathbf{g}^{\prime}\|-\|\mathbf{g}^{\prime}\|)^{2}\;.

This is now an optimization over a scalar, so we can again take the limit inside the minimization. We proceed to compute the limits of the two norms involving
\mathbf{g}^{\prime}
. Recycling the computation in (
128
), we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{g}^{\prime}\|^{2}\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(r_1+r_2)^2\sigma_1^2\tau_1^2}{mk}$}}\,\Big\|\Sigma_{\rm new}^{1/2}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2\sigma_1 \tau_1 \lambda}{2m}$}}\Sigma^{\dagger}+I_{p}\Big)^{\dagger}(P_{\Sigma}-\Sigma_{*})\mathbf{g}_{1}\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{r_2^2 \sigma_2^2\tau_2^2}{n}$}}\,\Big\|\Sigma_{\rm new}^{1/2}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2\sigma_2 \tau_2 \lambda}{2m}$}}\Sigma^{\dagger}+I_{p}\Big)^{\dagger}P_{*}\mathbf{g}_{2}\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta^2 \sigma_2^2\tau_2^2}{p}$}}\Big\|\Sigma_{\rm new}^{1/2}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2\sigma_2 \tau_2 \lambda}{2m}$}}\Sigma^{\dagger}+I_{p}\Big)^{\dagger}v(\beta^{*})\Big\|^{2}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

\displaystyle\;\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\rightarrow}

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(r_1+r_2)^2\sigma_1^2\tau_1^2}{k}$}}\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{21}+r_{2}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{2}\tau_{2}^{2}\,\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{22}+\theta^{2}\bar{\kappa}_{*}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{2}\tau_{2}^{2}\,\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{23}\;=\;\bar{\chi}^{r,\theta,\sigma,\tau}_{2}\;,

where we have denoted

\displaystyle\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{21}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\lim\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\big\|
\Sigma_{\rm new}^{1/2} (\Sigma^\dagger)^{1/2}
\big( \frac{2\sigma_1 \tau_1 \lambda}{2m} \Sigma^\dagger+ I_p \big)^\dagger(P_\Sigma- \Sigma_*)
\big\|^2
}{m}$}}\;,

\displaystyle\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{22}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\lim\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\big\|
\Sigma_{\rm new}^{1/2} (\Sigma^\dagger)^{1/2}
\Big( \mfrac{2\sigma_2 \tau_2 \lambda}{2m} \Sigma^\dagger+ I_p \Big)^\dagger\Sigma_*
\big\|^2
}{m}$}}\;,

\displaystyle\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{23}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\lim\,\Big\|\Sigma_{\rm new}^{1/2}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2\sigma_2 \tau_2 \lambda}{2m}$}}\Sigma^{\dagger}+I_{p}\Big)^{\dagger}P_{*}\Big\|^{2}\;.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\;\Big\|\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2m}$}}\Sigma^{\dagger}+\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}\Big)^{1/2}\Sigma^{1/2}(\Sigma_{\rm new}^{\dagger})^{1/2}\mathbf{g}^{\prime}\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\Big\|\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2m}$}}\Sigma^{\dagger}+\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}\Big)^{1/2}\Sigma^{1/2}P_{\Sigma_{\rm new}}(\Sigma^{\dagger})^{1/2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\lambda}{2m}$}}\Sigma^{\dagger}+\tilde{\Sigma}_{\sigma,\tau}\Big)^{\dagger}\big(\tilde{\mathbf{g}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\theta}{\sqrt{p}}$}}v(\beta^{*})\big)\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\rightarrow}

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(r_1+r_2)^2\sigma_1^2\tau_1^2}{k}$}}\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{31}+r_{2}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{2}\tau_{2}^{2}\,\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{32}+\theta^{2}\bar{\kappa}_{*}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{2}\tau_{2}^{2}\,\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{33}\;=\;\bar{\chi}^{r,\theta,\sigma,\tau}_{3}\;,

where we used

\displaystyle\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{31}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\lim\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\big\|
\big( \frac{\lambda}{2m} \Sigma^\dagger+ \tilde\Sigma_{\sigma, \tau}
\big)^{1/2}
\Sigma^{1/2} P_{\Sigma_{\rm new}}
(\Sigma^\dagger)^{1/2}
\big( \frac{2\sigma_1 \tau_1 \lambda}{2m} \Sigma^\dagger+ I_p \big)^\dagger(P_\Sigma- \Sigma_*)
\big\|^2
}{m}$}}\;,

\displaystyle\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{32}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\lim\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\big\|
\big( \frac{\lambda}{2m} \Sigma^\dagger+ \tilde\Sigma_{\sigma, \tau}
\big)^{1/2}
\Sigma^{1/2} P_{\Sigma_{\rm new}}
(\Sigma^\dagger)^{1/2}
\Big( \mfrac{2\sigma_2 \tau_2 \lambda}{2m} \Sigma^\dagger+ I_p \Big)^\dagger\Sigma_*
\big\|^2
}{m}$}}\;,

\displaystyle\bar{\chi}^{\sigma,\tau}_{33}\;\coloneqq
